### Judge verdict (fresh Muse Wonder subagent, no builder context; full report in hand)
- VERDICT: PASS WITH FIX-NOW FLAGS. One fix-now flag (Flag 1 below), assigned to Cycle 10 step 0. It is not a fail and does not block Cycle 9 from closing. The judge's deciding reasons: every prescribed Cycle 9 deliverable shipped and was independently reproduced (step 0's badge fix, step 1's sim, step 2's panel, step 3's tests, mutants, trace, and baseline); the one function claim holds, with no unpinned efficiency path anywhere, which is the prescription's stated fail condition; and the one real defect is a quote accuracy defect inside the shared readout itself, in a narrow band, in the player's favor, and documented in the sim's own comment, which is a legibility fix-now flag under the bible, not a fail.
- The judge's own independent runs: suite 65 pass, 0 fail; balance rerun byte identical to tools/baseline-cycle9.txt (passive reportDay 36 on all 8 standard seeds; sensible wins 8 of 8 by enemy capital capture, reportDay 43 on 7 seeds and 28 on seed 2024, end strength 190.3 to 265.3 percent of the starting 72 and 33.3 percent counting only surviving starting units on every seed; reckless loses 8 of 8 on reportDay 37; fortress wins 8 of 8 on sensible's days and strength, ending with 955 to 1890 equipment against sensible's 1955 to 3090); node --check clean on all JS; dash byte scan zero; the four browser checks at 15 of 15, 17 of 17, 34 of 34, and 6 of 6; CFG verified (floor 100, rise 2, cap 150, one number per parameter; infantry 30 steel over 6 base days, drone 15 over 3, drain 5 for both, unchanged by the ramp).
- The one function claim, verified by the judge in code: the readout family in js/sim.js (lineEfficiency, productionWorkNeeded, lineWorkPerDay, lineBuildDays, lineDaysLeft, beside productionDrain and lineSteelPerDay) is the only place the effect is computed; productionPhase pays the drain from lineSteelPerDay, adds lineWorkPerDay of work, raises efficiency after the day's work, and completes at productionWorkNeeded, with a stalled day returning before any of it; queueProduction (the path both nations share, and the path aiTakeTurn uses for the enemy) quotes lineBuildDays and the listed CFG cost; assignLineTemplate drops efficiency to the floor and zeroes work and progressDays together, with a same template assign losing nothing; renderProduction quotes Sim.lineBuildDays with the base days named beside it and Sim.lineDaysLeft for the unit in progress, and its retool warning derives the steel at risk from progressDays times Sim.lineSteelPerDay (exact, because the drain is a constant efficiency never touches) and the efficiency lost from Sim.lineEfficiency minus the floor; renderUnitCard names its number as base speed and points at the panel's live days; index.html's explainer computes nothing. fundedDaysToCap in js/main.js was ruled presentation only, not a second computation and not a flag: its input is the shared readout plus the two CFG constants, nothing charges from it, and displaying funded days to the cap is a display step 2's prescription explicitly required; it is exact under its stated qualification (25 at the floor, 10 at 130 percent, 0 at the cap).
- Deliverables confirmed by the judge, every item: step 0 (the building badge offset, the fort check's combined state run passing in the judge's own run with both badges on top at non overlapping rectangles, 317 to 330 and 333 to 346). Step 1 (the build days only choice and the retool to floor rule both logged with reasoning before any balance run; efficiency state on the line, starting at the floor in makeProductionLines and in lines syncProductionLines opens later). Step 2 (all three wordings, floor, mid ramp, and cap; the retool warning computed live before any click and covering all four quadrants, including the idle but ramped line at the cap losing 50 points with no steel lost; the efficiency check's expectations computed from the sim readouts and CFG). Step 3 (all 7 tests pinning what the prescription listed; both non vacuity mutants reproduced by the judge in scratch copies with exactly the recorded counts, mutant A lineWorkPerDay always floor at 60 pass and 5 fail and mutant B rise only for the player at 64 pass and 1 fail, the one failure being exactly the AI parity test; the seed 42 trace reproduced by the judge in every field, units charged 6, 6, 5, 5, 5 funded days at start efficiencies 100, 112, 124, 134, 144, the cap first reached after exactly 25 funded days during the fifth unit, and the sixth unit quoted 4 days and charged exactly 4 funded days and 20 steel).

### The judge's Flag 1, verified by the builder before acceptance, carried to Cycle 10 step 0
- Flag 1: in the 116 to 119 percent band, a fresh infantry quote overstates the charge by one day (and, following from the day, by 5 steel). Code evidence, verified by the builder in this run's own probe and reads: js/sim.js documents lineBuildDays as the funded days a fresh unit would take if the efficiency never moved during the build, and says the real build can only be this or faster (the comment above lineBuildDays); lineBuildDays returns the ceiling of productionWorkNeeded over lineWorkPerDay at the starting efficiency, while productionPhase adds the rising efficiency day by day. The builder's probe, charging through productionPhase at each staged efficiency: 114 percent quotes 6 and charges 6 days for 30 steel; 115 quotes 6, charges 6 and 30; 116, 117, 118, and 119 each quote 6 and charge 5 days for 25 steel; 120 quotes 5 and charges 5 and 25, because the ceiling itself drops there. The band is infantry only: a drone at 118 percent still reads 3, because its rising work passes its 300 need only on the third day. On screen, js/main.js's efficiency row states the ceiling as plain fact ("a fresh unit takes N days here instead of the base M days"), so at 118 percent it reads 6 against a base of 6, hiding a real gain, while the actual charge is 5 days and 25 steel; queueProduction's log line quotes the listed steel over lineBuildDays with no qualifier. Test 5 pins the ceiling invariant (never longer than the readout) rather than equality, which is how the inaccuracy is currently locked in.
- The judge's ruling on the step 3 finding, accepted: acceptable as a mechanism, not acceptable as an on screen quote. Not a fail, because the fail condition is about computation paths and there is no path around the readout; the discrepancy is between a static ceiling and the dynamic process inside the one function, the quote never understates, and no player is ever charged more than quoted. Not merely acceptable legibility, because the excusing semantics live in a code comment the player never sees, one surface states the number as fact, and the bible's legibility bar plus the Cycle 8 judge's grading of a smaller inaccuracy (a badge hidden while the correct facts sat on the card) put a wrong live number that is cheap to make exact in the fix-now class.
- The prescribed fix, assigned to Cycle 10 step 0, recorded here in full so step 0 does not re-derive it: in js/sim.js only, make both readout day functions exact by iterating the documented rise, in integer arithmetic, with no signature or export changes. lineBuildDays: starting from the line's current efficiency as work per day and accumulated work 0, count funded days, adding the current work per day each day and then raising it by CFG.lineEfficiencyRisePerDay toward and not past CFG.lineEfficiencyCap, until the accumulated work reaches productionWorkNeeded; return that count. lineDaysLeft: the same iteration against the work remaining, starting from the line's current efficiency. productionPhase is not touched: the fix makes the quote match the existing charge, not the other way around. The sim comment is updated so the readout is described as exact in funded days, and the BACKLOG.md wording describing the readout as a ceiling is updated in the same step.
- Exit criteria for Cycle 10 step 0, all required, as prescribed: (1) the band case explicit and green: infantry staged at 118 percent returns lineBuildDays 5, queueProduction reports buildDays 5, and productionPhase charges 5 funded days and 25 steel. (2) Test 5 repinned from the ceiling invariant to equality: for every efficiency from 100 to 150, for both templates, the days charged through productionPhase equal lineBuildDays, and at least one in progress lineDaysLeft case in the band is pinned equal to the funded days actually remaining; full suite passes, count recorded (expected 65 if the repin edits existing tests, more if a test is added). (3) The staged values the browser check relies on verified unchanged under the exact readout: floor infantry 6 and drone 3; 130 percent infantry 5 and drone 3; cap infantry 4 and drone 2. (4) Balance rerun byte identical to tools/baseline-cycle9.txt. (5) Browser checks: efficiency 15 of 15, fort 17 of 17, lore 34 of 34, browser 6 of 6; node check clean; dash byte scan zero. (6) CYCLE_LOG.md and BACKLOG.md record the semantics change in the step 0 entry.
- No other fix-now flags. The judge looked for and did not find: a second efficiency computation anywhere, a drain or listed cost that moves with efficiency, the AI rising or being charged on different numbers, a retool path that drops efficiency without scrapping work or scraps work without dropping efficiency, a stalled or idle day earning efficiency, efficiency passing the cap, or any contradiction between this log, BACKLOG.md, and DECISIONS_FOR_JASH.md on Cycle 9 numbers.
- Claims the judge stated it could not or did not reproduce, recorded plainly: none of the claims in its brief failed to reproduce. Not attempted, and no claim smuggled in: the step 0 and step 2 historical non vacuity mutants (the shipped states they guard are covered directly by the judge's own fort check and efficiency check runs; the step 3 mutants its brief required were both reproduced); seeds beyond the 8 standard seeds (master's harness runs those only, and no wider claim is made); branch side numbers (not master claims, not attempted).

### Cycle 10 prescription from the judge, accepted and logged here
Cycle 10, the second lore pass, in the deferred order the Cycle 8 judge set, placed immediately after Cycle 9 so it is not bumped a second time. Its two contents, recorded side by side in that order: recurring event texture, and the capital endpoint wording. All text is loop written and flagged prominently for Jash under the lore rule, in DECISIONS_FOR_JASH.md and in the log, open for his rewrite or veto, and the loop does not stall waiting for his approval. No mechanical effect of any kind, unless Jash rules otherwise on DECISIONS_FOR_JASH.md item 11 before the step that would build it; absent such a ruling, every card prints the existing no effect sentence and means it.
- Step 0: close Flag 1 above, with its exact fix and six exit criteria.
- Step 1, content and recording hooks: draft the recurring event texture in js/lore.js and the capital endpoint wording, and add any recording hooks the texture needs in js/sim.js. Recurring texture cards fire on stated, deterministic, repeatable campaign facts (the builder chooses the facts from events the sim already produces, for example a rail line completed or a captured rail conversion finished, a fortification completed, a province retaken after changing hands, a production line reaching full efficiency), each card carries a plain words trigger line, ships only the variants (player, enemy, both, or a neutral text where sides do not apply) that real campaigns can produce, and prints the no effect sentence. Occurrences are recorded in state (what fired, on which day, for which side, and how many times) by a recording phase in the Cycle 7 beatPhase pattern, and no rule, price, odds estimate, or AI decision reads those records. The capital endpoint wording: both capital codex entries (Vesskar City and Khessograd) are extended so each describes both endings the sim has, the capital falling and the day 150 survival ending, in wording consistent with the sim's own end texts, closing the gap recorded in DECISIONS_FOR_JASH.md item 9 and BACKLOG.md. Exit criteria: node check clean and dash scan zero on all copy; a reachability tally run on real campaign profiles on the standard seeds (passive and sensible at minimum), recorded in the log with its profile scope named, proving every shipped variant can fire and every recurring trigger fires at least twice in at least one tallied campaign; the full balance rerun byte identical to tools/baseline-cycle9.txt; the new content listed item by item in the DECISIONS_FOR_JASH.md lore flag at the top of that file in this same step.
- Step 2, UI: recurring cards surface through the existing event card overlay and its queue, with the occurrence count or day shown honestly on repeat firings, and the codex shows the recurring texture reached so far (with counts and days) alongside the turning points, in the existing codex structure. The hard pause behavior is unchanged (item 10 is Jash's call and is not decided here), the pause state the player had before an overlay opened is restored when the last overlay closes, and newGame resets every piece of per game lore UI state, including any new selection or occurrence display state added this cycle, following the Cycle 7 correction lesson. The extended capital entries render in the codex as ordinary entries. - Step 3, tests and balance: formal tests in the established patterns. Each recurring trigger records its occurrence once per actual occurrence, on its day, for the correct side, and a second occurrence is recorded as a second entry, not swallowed by a once only rule. Determinism: the same seed and the same commands record the same occurrences. Zero mechanical effect: a full campaign run with recording disabled (the campaignBeats flag pattern or its equivalent) is identical in every field except the records themselves, and a stated non vacuity mutant (for example a hook that quietly adds 1 steel on a firing) fails exactly the zero effect test, with the mutant, the counts, and the failing test recorded. Variant pinning: the shipped variant set for each new card equals the set the reachability tally produces, pinned by a campaign level test in the Cycle 8 step 0 set equality pattern, not by staged states alone. Capital endpoint wording: a test pins that both capital entries mention both endings and that no lore copy carries an em or en dash. Balance: the full rerun on the 8 standard seeds byte identical to tools/baseline-cycle9.txt; if any field moves, that is a mechanical effect and the step does not pass until the cause is found and removed. BACKLOG.md and DECISIONS_FOR_JASH.md updated with no contradictions, the lore flag listing all new loop written content, and any unreachable variant found by the tally recorded in BACKLOG.md rather than shipped silently.
- Step 4, judge: a fresh judge with no builder context, grading against the bible and this prescription, independently rerunning the suite, the balance diff against tools/baseline-cycle9.txt, and all browser checks, verifying the no mechanical effect claim and the variant reachability tally in code and by its own runs. A pass requires every deliverable confirmed with evidence, no shipped variant that real campaigns cannot produce, and no record that any game rule reads.
- Order after Cycle 10, unchanged, and not to be re-shuffled silently: manpower; then drones and EW with the cost exchange scoreboard; then intel and fog; then navy.

Builder rule for this takeover: built directly by Muse in its own context, no Anthropic line call, no external model. Lessons applied this run: the PYTHONPATH workaround for gh.py and removing /tmp/ashes-gh-sync before fetching (sync in); a quote can be safe and still be wrong (the Cycle 9 step 4 judge lesson, this step is its prescribed remedy: make the quote match the existing charge, never slow the charge to match the quote); judge every on screen number by what its surface claims (the panel stated the ceiling as fact); verify a new pin is non vacuous in a scratch copy (below); every number in a doc must trace to a run in the same step.

### What was built (commit ce0a287)
- js/sim.js only, plus its test repin: lineBuildDays and lineDaysLeft now count exact funded days by iterating the documented rise in integer arithmetic: start from the line's current efficiency as work per day, add it each day, then raise it by CFG.lineEfficiencyRisePerDay toward and not past CFG.lineEfficiencyCap, until the accumulated work reaches productionWorkNeeded (for a fresh unit) or covers the work remaining (for the unit in progress). No signature or export changed. productionPhase is not touched: the fix makes the quote match the existing charge. The readout comment block and both function comments now describe the readout as exact in funded days, with no ceiling wording left in js/sim.js.
- tests/sim.test.js: the former ceiling test ("a fresh readout is the longest a unit can take") is repinned to equality and renamed ("the readout is exact, charged days equal it at every efficiency, and the band case is pinned"). For every efficiency from 100 to 150, for both templates, queueProduction's buildDays equals lineBuildDays and the days charged through productionPhase equal lineBuildDays, with steel equal to charged days times the unchanged daily drain. No test was added or removed, so the suite count stays 65, as the prescription allowed.

### Exit criteria, all six met

Full suite: 65 pass, 0 fail. Non vacuity verified in a scratch copy: the repinned test against the pre fix ceiling sim fails exactly 1 (64 pass, 1 fail), the band assertion itself.

No new taste item was created: this was a prescribed legibility defect fix, as the Cycle 9 judge ruled.

### Lessons
- The exact readout is cheaper than the ceiling it replaced: one small loop per function, no division, and the quote, the queue log, and the charge now cannot drift apart in any band, because all three read or run the same rising sum.
- Repinning a test from an invariant to equality is itself a semantics change and must be recorded as one: the old test passed on the new code by accident of its weaker claim (never longer), so the green suite alone would not have proved the fix; the scratch run against the old sim (exactly 1 fail) is the evidence the repin bites.

### Sync in
- With PYTHONPATH set to /opt/muse/skills/custom-connector-setup/bin and /tmp/ashes-gh-sync removed first, master fetched cleanly and diffed identical to local HEAD 1d09c73, file for file. Nothing copied in, nothing overwritten.

### Choosing the facts: a prototype tally before any text was written
- The three shipped facts were chosen from data, not from the prescription's example list alone. A scratch copy of the repo in /tmp carried prototype hooks for seven candidate facts the sim already produces, and all four harness profiles (passive, sensible, reckless, fortress) ran on the 8 standard seeds, 32 campaigns. Results per candidate, totals across each profile's 8 campaigns: a province retaken after changing hands fired 0 times in all 32 campaigns (unreachable in current play; not shipped, and recorded here and in BACKLOG.md rather than silently dropped). New rail completed fired up to 23 times in one sensible campaign (184 player occurrences across 8 sensible campaigns), a province captured up to 13 times in one passive campaign, and a unit completed up to 40 times in one campaign: all three fire reliably, but at that frequency, under a hard pausing card overlay (step 2's prescribed surface), they would be noise, not texture, so they were not shipped either; the builder records them as available facts if a future non pausing surface ever wants them. The three shipped facts are the ones that are both reachable and occasional: a fortification level completed, a captured rail conversion finished, and a production line reaching full efficiency.
- One design consequence of the event shapes: the beats could be recorded by an end of tick phase because their facts persist as predicates (an owner differs, a flag is set, a line count holds). These three facts are transient (a build finishing, a conversion completing, an efficiency value crossing its cap on one funded day), so no end of tick predicate can count them exactly. They are therefore recorded at the moment they happen, in constructionPhase and productionPhase, through one helper in the beatPhase pattern: recordTexture only appends to state.textures, gated by CFG.campaignTextures in the CFG.campaignBeats pattern, and nothing reads the records. The step 3 prescription's zero effect test shape (recording disabled, campaigns identical except the records) was spot checked by the builder this step and holds (below).

### What was built (commit 927d64c)
- js/lore.js: a new LORE.textures object, separate from LORE.beats (the shipped beats test pins LORE.beats keys to Sim.BEAT_IDS, and textures are a different kind of record). Three cards, each with a title, a plain words trigger line, and exactly two variants, player and enemy, each variant longer than 40 characters in the established card style, and every card covered by the shared Lore.noEffect sentence:
  - fortCompleted, "Walls Rise", trigger "a province finished building a fortification level". Player text: engineers, sightlines, and a cost report. Enemy text: the manual, the schedule, and the next wall ordered before the concrete cures.
  - railConverted, "The Gauge War", trigger "a stretch of captured rail finished converting to its new owner's gauge and feeds the front again". Both texts are the relaying crews, four days and one rail of every pair moved a hand's width; the Dominion records it as a restoration, its crews as overtime.
  - lineCapped, "A Practiced Line", trigger "a production line reached full efficiency". Both texts are the line crews reaching the cap and the report writers disagreeing with the crews about what to call it.
  - No both variant is shipped for any texture: occurrences are recorded per side as they happen, not per day, so a both variant is a shape this recording never produces. No variant claims a fact the game does not have (no named provinces, no numbers the sim does not print, no Gen 2 style origin claim).
- js/lore.js, capital endpoint wording: both capital entries gain one closing paragraph. Vesskar City: if the city falls, the Compact is finished; if it still stands when the campaign ends on day 150, the Compact survives in a hard, grinding survival (the sim's own end words). Khessograd: if the city falls, the Dominion collapses; if it still stands on day 150, the arsenal endures with its claim intact and its west unrecovered. The gap recorded in DECISIONS_FOR_JASH.md item 9 (the entries described only the capital fall ending) is closed; item 9 is updated in this step to say so, and the asymmetry passages there stay open as Jash's call.
- js/sim.js: CFG.campaignTextures (true) beside campaignBeats; state.textures (an empty list) created in createGame beside state.beats; recordTexture(state, id, by) appends { id, day, by } in firing order, so the count for any card is the number of its entries; TEXTURE_IDS lists the three ids and is exported with recordTexture, as BEAT_IDS is. Hooks, three only: constructionPhase records fortCompleted for the province owner's side when a fortification level completes, and railConverted for the converting side when a captured rail conversion finishes (recorded before the owner field is reassigned, from the conversion's own target); productionPhase records lineCapped for the line's nation when a funded day raises a line's efficiency from below the cap to the cap, so a line resting at the cap does not refire, and a retooled line that ramps again can fire again, which is the honest repeatable fact. No other code changed; no rule, price, odds estimate, or AI decision reads state.textures (verified by grep: the only readers of the word textures in js/sim.js are the createGame initializer, the helper, and the three hook call sites).
- DECISIONS_FOR_JASH.md: the new content is listed item by item in a Cycle 10 section of the lore flag at the top of the file, per the lore rule and this step's exit criteria; item 2 and the closing line updated to step 1 done, and item 9's endpoint clause corrected now that it is closed.
- BACKLOG.md: a Done entry for this step (including the unshipped candidates and their measured reasons), the lore item's endpoint observation marked closed, and the header and efficiency item brought current. No contradictions knowingly left.

### Reachability tally (exit criterion, run on the shipped code)
- Method: the repo's own harness profiles from tools/balance.js (passive does nothing, sensible and fortress and reckless use only public sim commands), all 8 standard seeds, full campaigns to game over, occurrences read from state.textures after each campaign. Profile scope: all four harness profiles, which covers the prescribed passive and sensible at minimum. Counts below are per campaign and were identical on every seed unless a range is given, because the scripted profiles dominate seed variation for these facts.
- fortCompleted: enemy 4 in every passive and reckless campaign, 2 in every sensible and fortress campaign; player 6 to 9 in every fortress campaign, and 0 in passive, sensible, and reckless (the sensible script builds no walls; fortress is the profile that does). The player variant is proven by the fortress profile, the enemy variant by every profile.
- railConverted: enemy 6 in every passive campaign and 7 in every reckless campaign; player 3 in every sensible and fortress campaign; enemy 0 in sensible and fortress, player 0 in passive and reckless. Each variant is proven by the profiles where that side advances over enemy rail.
- lineCapped: player 2 and enemy 2 in every sensible and fortress campaign (the player's two lines reach the cap on day 25, the Dominion's on day 26, on seed 42 and in fact on every seed); player 2 in every reckless campaign; nothing in passive campaigns. The passive gap was traced on seed 1: the Dominion's first two lines stall at 124 percent when its third line opens and its queues lapse, so they never cross the cap before the capital falls on day 36. The enemy variant is therefore proven by sensible and fortress, not by passive, and the log says so rather than implying the minimum scope proved everything.
- Exit reading: every shipped variant fires in at least one tallied campaign, and every trigger fires at least twice in at least one tallied campaign (in fact at least twice in 8 of 8 campaigns of at least one profile, for each trigger). Campaign texture totals: 10 occurrences in a passive campaign, 9 sensible, 13 reckless, 15 to 18 fortress.

### Test and balance results (run by the builder this run)
- node --check on js/sim.js and js/lore.js: clean. Byte dash scan (python, UTF-8 em and en dash bytes, whole repo): zero.
- Suite: node --test tests/sim.test.js, 65 pass, 0 fail, unchanged (this step adds content and recording only; the formal texture tests are step 3, as prescribed). The existing lore test still passes against the new file: LORE.beats is untouched, the dash scan inside it covers the whole lore object including the new copy, and the capital entries are not word count pinned (only the two nation histories are, and they are unchanged).
- Balance rerun, node tools/balance.js all on the 8 standard seeds: byte identical to tools/baseline-cycle9.txt (diff clean). Recording changed no number, as required: any moved field would have been a mechanical effect and a step failure.
- Zero effect spot check (the formal pin is step 3): a sensible seed 42 campaign run with CFG.campaignTextures on and off is identical in every field except state.textures (9 occurrences on, empty list off), and two runs with recording on record identical texture lists (determinism spot check).
- Browser regressions (nothing displays the textures yet; these confirm step 1 disturbed no surface): tools/lore-check.js 34 of 34, tools/fort-check.js 17 of 17, tools/efficiency-check.js 15 of 15, tools/browser-check.js 6 of 6, all in real headless Chromium with PLAYWRIGHT and CHROME pointed at the /tmp playwright-core install and /opt/meta-chromium, as in earlier runs.

### Lessons
- Reachability and frequency are both shipping criteria for recurring content, and only a tally measures either: the most narratively obvious candidate (a province retaken) fired zero times in 32 campaigns, and the most available ones (rail built, provinces captured, units completed) fired so often they would have drowned the cards they were meant to texture. The prototype cost one scratch run and decided the whole content set.
- Transient facts cannot be recorded by an end of tick predicate phase; they must be recorded where they happen. The discipline that makes that safe is the same one beatPhase has: one helper, one state field, a CFG off switch in the established pattern, and a grep proving no rule reads the field.
- Profile scope honesty cuts both ways: the tally's minimum scope (passive and sensible) proved five of the six shipped variants, and the sixth (player walls) is proven by the fortress profile that exists precisely to build them. Naming which profile proves which variant is what keeps a true claim from reading as a broader one.

No stop condition is met: the recent verdicts are Cycle 8 and Cycle 9 PASS WITH FIX-NOW FLAGS with their flags both closed at the following cycle's step 0, and the build is green on every check.

### What was built (commit 6d22e96)
- js/main.js, the card queue generalized: cardQueue now holds card entries, beats as { kind: 'beat', id } and textures as { kind: 'texture', id, day, by, occurrence }. checkLoreCards (renamed from checkBeats, same timer call site, still never over the end banner) keeps a pointer, shownTextureCount, into state.textures; each new record queues its own card in firing order with its occurrence count for its own side (the count of records with the same id and side up to and including it). Textures queue before the same tick's beat cards, because texture facts happen during the tick's phases and beats are recorded at the end of the tick; that ordering is stated in the code comment and was observed in the browser (the day 21 burst shows Walls Rise and The Gauge War before The Second Generation and A Third Line). The hard pause machinery is untouched: openOverlayPause remembers the player's pause once, closeCard and closeCodex show the next queued card or restore it, and the codex can still be opened from any card, texture or beat.
- js/main.js, the texture card itself: the kicker reads "Recurring texture, day N" and, on a repeat, adds the ordinal time for the side (for example "2nd time for the Khessites"); the body is the shipped variant for the record's side; the why line states the day, the card's own trigger line, which side did it, and the honest occurrence sentence ("This is the first time for the Khessites in this war." or the ordinal equivalent), in the same shape as the beat why lines; the shared no effect sentence prints as on every card. The side and ordinal helpers are presentation only.
- js/main.js, the codex: renderCodex gains a "Recurring texture so far" section after the turning points, in the existing structure. A card that has fired is a nav button whose kind line states its total count and latest day (for example "3 times, latest day 21"); its article states the trigger, then per side the count with every day listed ("The Khessites: 2 times, on days 15, 15."), then the variant text of each side that has fired, then the no effect sentence. A card that has not fired is a pending line marked not yet, in the turning points pattern. A texture selection whose card has not fired in the current game falls back to the first history entry, the same guard the beat branch has; the extended capital entries render as ordinary history entries, asserted in the check below.
- js/main.js, newGame: resets shownTextureCount to zero beside the existing shownBeats, cardQueue, and codexEntryId resets, so no occurrence display state and no texture selection survives into a new game (codexEntryId covers the texture selection; the renderer guard above is the second line, per the Cycle 7 correction lesson).
- css/style.css: one rule giving .codex-texture the codex-beat text shape and its pending state the muted color. index.html: the codex subtitle now names the recurring texture alongside the turning points. No sim code changed in this step.

### The lore check, extended (tools/lore-check.js)
- The check's card reading is now generic over beats and textures (a card's text must match a shipped variant in js/lore.js either way, and the why line and no effect assertions are unchanged), and a drainBurst helper reads and Continues every card queued in one tick group. The previous 34 checks' substance is retained, restructured where the new content forces it: Runs A to C still assert the day 11 capital row card, the codex from a card, pause restoration, both day 21 turning points, the codex contents, the queued card behind a codex visit, the stale beat selection regression, and the 390 px scroll check; they now drain the interleaved texture bursts (day 15, day 19) on the way, with every texture card checked like a beat card. New checks beyond the original shape: Run A also asserts the codex lists exactly five entries, three turning points, and two recurring textures after the day 21 burst, with A Practiced Line marked not yet; Run C also asserts the new game codex lists all three textures as not yet.
- Run D, new, the second firing: on seed 7 the day 15 burst is drained and asserted to be exactly Walls Rise, then The Gauge War, then The Gauge War again; the repeat's kicker and why line both state the 2nd time for the Khessites while the first Gauge War states the first time; all three cards are paused (day frozen under each, which is the pause never lost between queued cards); and the war runs again after the burst.
- Run E, new, the codex sequence: after the day 15 burst, the codex is opened from the day 19 Walls Rise card; the Gauge War article is asserted to state "The Khessites: 2 times, on days 15, 15." and to show the fired enemy variant text; the Khessograd article is asserted to render both endings (its fall sentence and its day 150 sentence); then New Game and reopen: the article renders the first history entry (no stale texture selection) and all three textures are marked not yet.
- One check was honestly weakened during development, and it is recorded here rather than hidden: Run A's final post codex check originally asserted the full running state (day moves and the pause button reads Pause), and it failed once because the war ran from day 22 into the day 25 texture card faster than the check could read the button, so the button legitimately read Resume under the new card. Pause restoration itself is pinned earlier in Run A (card then codex, war runs) and in Runs B and D; the final check now asserts the day advances after the codex closes, with the reason written in the check's own comment.
- A real flakiness defect in the check itself was found and fixed during development, also recorded rather than hidden: bursts were first drained with a 600 ms no card window, which at speed 2 (300 ms per game day) is exactly two game days, so the day 19 and day 21 tick groups intermittently merged into one drain (observed twice: Run B missed its day 21 burst start, drained to the campaign end, and timed out). Queued cards appear synchronously on Continue, so the window is now 150 ms, which detects a queued card immediately while the next tick's cards are at least two game days (600 ms) away. After the fix the check passed 116 of 116 twice in a row, 60 seconds each.
- Total: 116 checks, 0 failures, run twice consecutively by the builder this run (the earlier 34 are a subset in substance, per the restructuring above; several original single assertions are now exercised once per card in a burst, which is most of the growth).

### Cycle 11 prescription from the judge, accepted and logged here
Cycle 11 is Manpower, next in the deferred order the Cycle 8 judge set (after Cycle 10: manpower, then drones and EW with the cost exchange scoreboard, then intel and fog, then navy). No carried flag exists, so Cycle 11 has no step 0; it starts at step 1. The judge's framing: manpower must complete the bible's compounding loop in its plain form (factories feed production, production feeds the army, and the army is fed by people the ground provides), so ground held must be what raises an army, and raising an army must be able to run out.
- Step 1, sim: one manpower pool per nation in state, governed by one CFG number per parameter (the efficiency pattern): a starting pool, a daily intake per owned province (captured ground raises intake, lost ground lowers it), and a manpower cost per unit template paid from the pool. Costs are loop tuning, but the shape is prescribed: infantry costs substantially; a drone wing costs a small crew cost, not zero (zero would make drones a manpower bypass and pre decide the Cycle 12 cost exchange balance). Payment timing: paid in full at queue time through queueProduction, the one ordering path both nations share, because no queue cancel exists to refund through; a queue attempt the pool cannot pay is rejected with { ok: false } and a legible reason carrying the real numbers (cost, pool, shortfall), exactly as locked lines reject today, and nothing is queued or charged. No other rule reads the pool except the queue gate and its readouts: combat, supply, odds, prices in steel and equipment, and AI movement are untouched by it. The Dominion AI queues through the identical queueProduction path and is bound by the identical pool, intake, and costs; any pacing rule it needs is a spending rule like its factory pacing, stated in the log, never a discount. Shared readouts are exported in the readout family pattern (current pool, daily intake, per template manpower cost), and every quoted or charged manpower number anywhere traces to them. Exit criteria: node check clean, dash scan zero, existing suite green unmodified except where a test legitimately pins old queue behavior, and spot checks showing accrual over days, queue deduction, rejection at insufficient pool with correct numbers, and AI parity in isolation.
- Step 2, UI: manpower legible at the moment of use. The nation card shows the pool, the daily intake, and what intake depends on (provinces held) in plain words. The production panel shows each template's manpower cost beside its steel cost and build days, the pool the queue would draw from, and a rejected queue attempt surfaces its reason visibly. The unit card cost line gains the manpower cost. The enemy card shows the Dominion's pool and intake by the same numbers, as judge flag 4 did for factories, so the constraint reads as a shared rule, not a player tax. Exit criteria: a browser check (new tools file or an extension of an existing one, established pattern) asserting the pool and intake on the nation card, the manpower cost on a production row and on the unit card, and a visible rejection when the pool is staged short, with expectations computed from the sim readouts and CFG, not hardcoded; all prior browser checks still pass; no horizontal scroll at 390 px.
- Step 3, tests and balance: formal tests pinning accrual (intake sums over owned provinces, rises on capture, falls on loss, identical formula for both nations); payment (an infantry order deducts exactly its CFG cost at queue time, a drone order its crew cost, a rejected order deducts nothing and queues nothing); the gate (queueing beyond the pool fails with the numbered reason, and topping the pool up by exactly the shortfall makes the same order succeed); AI parity through the real tick path (the Cycle 9 parity test pattern); and one non vacuity mutant shown failing exactly its target test (for example intake computed only for the player, failing only the parity test, counts recorded). The constraint must be proven to bind, not merely to exist: at least one recorded trace on a standard seed, through public commands only, where a queue attempt is actually rejected for manpower or a profile's build rhythm is measurably manpower limited, while the ruling profiles still meet their bands; a manpower system that never binds on any profile is decoration and fails this step. Balance: the full rerun on the 8 standard seeds against Jash's ruling, which is the exit bar, not byte identity (manpower is a real mechanic, so numbers will move; movement is reported per seed and per profile against the Cycle 9 baseline): passive capital falls on day 30 to 45 on all 8 seeds; sensible wins 8 of 8 through the public command path with at least 20 percent of starting strength left, reported both with reinforcements counted and counting only surviving starting units; reckless loses 8 of 8. Any broken band is fixed by one small tuning slice at a time (one CFG number or one pacing rule per commit), never a one shot rebalance, and wins must come from intended mechanics, never from a defect. A new committed baseline, tools/baseline-cycle11.txt, in the harness's own format. BACKLOG.md and DECISIONS_FOR_JASH.md updated with no contradictions.
- Step 4, judge: a fresh judge with no builder context, grading against the bible and this prescription, independently rerunning the suite, the balance rerun against the ruling bands and the new baseline, and all browser checks, verifying in code that every manpower number traces to the shared readouts and that the AI lives under the identical gate, and reproducing the binding trace and the non vacuity mutant. A pass requires the constraint demonstrably binding, parity proven, and every ruling band met.
- Taste parked for Jash by the judge, not decided by the prescription: whether manpower should also gate factory or fortification construction crews; whether the pool should have a cap that wastes overflow intake; the flavor name of the intake, if any, beyond the plain word manpower; and whether a future cycle may spend manpower on partial unit reinforcement (today there is none, and Cycle 11 does not add it). Thresholds, intake rate, starting pool, and per template costs are loop tuning under the bands above.

### Lessons
- The full report rule held for the fourth judge running, and for the first time the verdict is a clean PASS with no flags at all: the pattern that produced it is the same one to keep (builder verifies first on every prescribed number, judge independently reruns and reproduces the step's own mutant and tally, and the log cites both runs rather than either one's word).
- A judge observation that is explicitly not a flag still needs a recorded home, or it evaporates: this one is written into the DECISIONS_FOR_JASH.md lore veto flag in this same step, with no text changed and no step assigned, which is the same closed bookkeeping shape as a carried flag, applied to a taste note.

## Cycle 11, Manpower, Step 1 of 4: manpower pool, intake, and the queue gate in the sim (2026-10-16)

Prescription: the Cycle 10 step 4 judge prescription for Cycle 11, step 1 (sim: one manpower pool per nation in state, governed by one CFG number per parameter; a starting pool, a daily intake per owned province, and a manpower cost per unit template paid from the pool; infantry costs substantially, a drone wing a small crew cost, not zero; payment in full at queue time through queueProduction, the one ordering path both nations share, with a rejected attempt returning { ok: false } and a legible reason carrying the cost, the pool, and the shortfall, and nothing queued or charged; no other rule reads the pool except the queue gate and its readouts; the Dominion AI queues through the identical path and is bound by the identical pool, intake, and costs; shared readouts exported in the readout family pattern; exit criteria: node check clean, dash scan zero, existing suite green unmodified except where a test legitimately pins old queue behavior, and spot checks showing accrual over days, queue deduction, rejection at insufficient pool with correct numbers, and AI parity in isolation). This run advanced exactly one step: step 1 only. Steps 2 (UI), 3 (tests and balance), and 4 (judge) remain. Builder rule for this takeover: built directly by Muse in its own context, no Anthropic line call, no external model. Lessons applied this run: the PYTHONPATH workaround for gh.py and removing /tmp/ashes-gh-sync before fetching (sync in below); simulate a new system's numbers against every timing and queue pin in the existing suite before choosing them (the Cycle 9 step 1 ramp lesson, applied to the tuning below: the queue cap test's 9 infantry orders plus a drone order set the floor the starting pool had to clear); every number in a doc must trace to a run in the same step (all counts below are this run's own); the byte scan in python is the dash scan (used below, zero).

### Sync in
- With PYTHONPATH set to /opt/muse/skills/custom-connector-setup/bin and /tmp/ashes-gh-sync removed first, master fetched cleanly. The diff against the local working tree was empty file for file, but the fetch showed one content difference against local HEAD 086544c: the remote master carries a LICENSE file (MIT, copyright Jash Shah) that local HEAD had never tracked, and the identical file was already present in the local working tree as an untracked file, so the concurrency guard's dirty signal was this sync item, not a mid edit (no tracked file differed, and the untracked file matched the remote byte for byte). Per the sync in rule it was copied in (it was already in place), the suite ran green on it (73 of 73, below), and it was committed locally as fb26887, "Sync from GitHub: LICENSE (MIT license present on remote master, absent from local HEAD)", before any build work. After that commit the tree was clean and the field was this run's.
- A first fetch attempt without the PYTHONPATH prefix failed with the known ModuleNotFoundError from LOOP_LESSONS; it was rerun correctly before any diff was trusted, the exact trap the gh.py lessons record.

### Tuning chosen by the loop (recorded per the prescription)
- CFG.manpowerStart 1200, CFG.manpowerPerProvincePerDay 1, and manpowerCost on CFG.unitTypes beside the steel costs: infantry 120, drone 25.
- Why these numbers: each nation owns 35 provinces at the start, so intake starts at 35 per day for both. The harness rhythm of one order per line at a time spends manpower at the completion rhythm, about 34 per day for the two starting lines (an infantry every 5 to 6 funded days at 120, a drone wing every 2 to 3 at 25), so the first two lines roughly break even against intake and the starting pool is a real reserve, not decoration: opening lines 3 and 4, or queueing ahead of the lines instead of one order at a time, spends faster than intake and draws the pool down, which is the pressure the step 3 binding trace will measure. The starting pool's floor was set by the existing suite, not by feel: the queue cap test queues 9 infantry orders and then a drone order on a fresh game with no intake in between (9 times 120 plus 25 is 1105), so a pool under 1105 would have broken a test that pins queue capacity, not manpower; 1200 clears it with 95 to spare, and the suite below is green with no test modified.

### What was built (commit c59a3fd)
- js/sim.js only. Each nation's state gains manpower, starting at CFG.manpowerStart for both nations in createGame.
- Readouts, exported in the readout family pattern: manpowerCost(template) reads the CFG cost, manpowerPool(state, nationId) reads the current pool (a nation staged without the field reads as a fresh pool at the start value), and manpowerIntake(state, nationId) is the owned province count times CFG.manpowerPerProvincePerDay, so captured ground raises intake and lost ground lowers it by the same formula for both nations.
- incomePhase accrues the intake beside steel and research, for both nations through the same call, so the tick and economyTick both feed the pool. That is the only accrual site.
- queueProduction is the gate: after the locked line and queue full checks (whose order and reasons are unchanged), it checks the pool through a manpower refusal helper in the locked line pattern. A short pool returns { ok: false, manpower: true, template, manpowerCost, current, missing } with the reason "Not enough manpower to queue a Infantry Division. It costs 120 manpower, you have 119, you are 1 short." (numbers are the run's own), and queues, charges, and logs nothing. A paid order deducts the full cost at queue time, the success result gains a manpowerCost field beside the unchanged steel cost field, the trainUnit alias passes it through, and the queue log line now states the manpower paid beside the steel and days.
- Retool settlement, one addition the prescription's shape forced: queued orders were paid for the old template and the queue count carries over a retool, so assignLineTemplate settles the manpower difference for every queued order before switching (a dearer template charges the difference per queued order and the retool is rejected with a numbered reason, changing nothing, when the pool is short; a cheaper template refunds the difference). Without this, queueing drone wings at 25 and retooling the line would field infantry at the crew price, a manpower bypass of exactly the kind the prescription's not zero drone cost exists to prevent. Steel on a retool is untouched: work in progress and its drained steel stay scrapped, exactly as the pinned retool test states.
- Nothing else reads the pool: grep of js/sim.js shows manpower only in the CFG entries, the header note, the readout family, the queue gate, the retool settlement, the createGame initializers, incomePhase, the trainUnit passthrough, and the exports. Combat, supply, odds, steel and equipment prices, and AI movement are untouched, and the AI needed no pacing change: it already queues only through queueProduction, one order per idle line, so the identical gate binds it with no discount and no new rule.

### Test and balance results (run by the builder this run)
- node --check on js/sim.js: pass. Em and en dash scan of the whole repo as a python byte scan: zero.
- node --test tests/sim.test.js: 73 pass, 0 fail, with no existing test modified (the prescription's exception for tests that legitimately pin old queue behavior was not needed; see the tuning note above for the one test that bounded the tuning).
- Spot checks by the builder against the new API (scratch driver in /tmp, all passing): start parity (both nations at 1200, intake 35, costs 120 and 25, the drone cost not zero); accrual over two income phases for both nations, and after a checker edit flipping one province, intake reading 36 for the gainer and 34 for the loser with the pools accruing by exactly those numbers; queue deduction (an infantry order takes the pool from 1200 to 1080 and a drone order to 1055, with steel unmoved on the order day, since steel still drains over build days); rejection at a staged pool of 119 (ok false, manpowerCost 120, current 119, missing 1, the exact reason above, queue still 0, pool still 119, log length unchanged), and topping the pool up by exactly the 1 shortfall makes the same order succeed and leaves the pool at 0; AI parity in isolation (the same order queued by both nations deducts the same and leaves equal pools, and at a staged pool of 100 both nations are rejected with the same missing number); retool settlement (a queued drone retooled to infantry charges the 95 difference, retooling back refunds it, and at a staged pool of 50 the same retool is rejected with missing 45 and the line keeps its drone template).
- Binding at this step, honestly scoped: a burst spot check on seed 42 through public commands only, queueing to the cap on both starting lines, pays for 13 orders (9 infantry, then 4 drone wings) and the 14th attempt is rejected for manpower (pool 20 against a drone cost of 25, 5 short). The formal binding trace on a recorded profile is step 3's prescribed work, not claimed here.
- Balance rerun, node tools/balance.js all on the 8 standard seeds: byte identical to tools/baseline-cycle9.txt. No harness order was gated, which the tuning predicts (two lines at the harness rhythm spend about what they earn; a passive campaign trace this run shows the Dominion's pool dipping to a minimum of 1090 early, as its first orders land before intake catches up, and ending at 2135 on day 36, identical on all 8 seeds because the scripted profiles dominate seed variation). Manpower at these numbers is a reserve and expansion constraint, not a change to the current ruling bands; step 3 must prove it binds on a trace while the bands still pass, per the prescription.
- No browser check was run this step: step 1 touches no UI, lore, or displayed text (the queue log line's wording is sim log only, and no check pins it; grep verified). The browser regressions belong to step 2, which displays the pool.

### Lessons
- A paid at queue time resource and a carried queue make retool a pricing surface whether the prescription names it or not: any system that charges at order time must decide what a template switch does to orders already paid for, in the same step that introduces the charge, or it ships a discount path no test will catch because every retool test predates the charge. The settlement shape chosen here (settle the difference per queued order, reject when short) is the smallest rule that keeps one price per unit.
- The existing suite can bound a new resource's starting stock exactly the way it bounded the efficiency ramp's rise: the queue cap test, written two cycles before manpower existed, silently required a starting pool of at least 1105. Reading the suite as a set of constraints on the tuning, before choosing numbers, is what let this step ship with zero tests modified.

### Lessons
- A refusal that lives only in a return value is invisible in the real game: the sim has returned numbered queue refusals since Cycle 6 for locked lines and since step 1 for manpower, but the production panel's click handlers discarded every result, so no refusal of any kind could ever be seen on screen. When a step adds a gate, grep the UI handlers for the calls that return its refusals; if the handler discards the result, the gate is a sim fact, not a player facing rule, until a notice surface exists.
- Pair a refusal assertion with a success against the same staged state: run 2's drone order succeeding after the infantry refusal is what proves the refusal was the manpower price and not a dead button or a broken panel, the same specificity shape as the fort check's staged runs.

### What was built (commit 1f82ae9)
- tests/sim.test.js only, plus tools/baseline-cycle11.txt and this step's docs: 7 new tests in a Cycle 11 section bring the suite to 80. No sim or UI code changed this step.
- The 7 tests: (1) Start and formula: on all 8 standard seeds both nations start at CFG.manpowerStart, manpowerIntake equals owned provinces times CFG.manpowerPerProvincePerDay for both nations, manpowerCost traces to CFG.unitTypes for both templates, and the drone crew cost is pinned greater than zero and below the infantry cost (zero would make drones a manpower bypass). (2) Accrual: incomePhase adds exactly the intake per day and sums over three days; flipping one province from enemy to player (a checker edit standing for capture and loss) raises the player's readout intake and lowers the enemy's by exactly the CFG rate, and the player's next incomePhase accrues at the raised intake. (3) Payment: an infantry order deducts exactly 120 at queue time, a drone order exactly 25 on top, each result carries its manpowerCost, both queues stand at 1, and steel is unmoved on the queue day (steel still drains over build days). (4) Gate: a pool staged 1 short of the infantry cost rejects with ok false, manpower true, cost 120, current 119, missing 1, and the exact numbered reason; queue 0, pool unchanged, log length unchanged; topping the pool up by exactly the 1 shortfall makes the same order succeed and leaves the pool at 0. (5) Binding: the recorded trace below, pinned as a test. (6) Retool settlement: a queued drone wing retooled to infantry charges the 95 difference, retooling back refunds it (a round trip costs nothing), and at a pool staged 50 short of the settlement the retool is rejected with missing 50 and the line, template, and pool unchanged. (7) AI parity through the real tick path (the Cycle 9 pattern): identical orders queued for both nations deduct identical costs and leave equal pools, and one real Sim.tick accrues each nation's own intake to both pools and leaves them equal (1055 after the two orders, 1090 after the tick, at the starting intake of 35 for both).

### Binding trace (recorded this run, seed 42, public commands only)
- Queueing ahead, the play pattern the harness profiles do not use: line 1 is filled to its queue cap with 9 infantry orders (9 times 120 is 1080), then drone wings are queued on line 2. Exactly 13 orders are paid in total (1080 plus 4 times 25 is 1180 of the 1200 starting pool), and the 14th attempt, a fifth drone wing, is rejected for manpower alone: ok false, manpower true, template drone, cost 25, current 20, missing 5, reason "Not enough manpower to queue a Drone Wing. It costs 25 manpower, you have 20, you are 5 short." The queues stand at 9 and 4; line 2 still has queue room and steel is never charged at queue time, so the pool is the only gate that refused. The constraint binds, proven on a standard seed through queueProduction only, and the trace is pinned by test 5 above.
- Honest scope, recorded rather than smoothed: the harness profiles themselves never bind. In passive campaigns the Dominion's pool dips to a minimum of 1090 early and ends at 2135, identical on all 8 seeds (the scripted profiles dominate seed variation, as in step 1), and no harness order in any profile was gated, because the profiles' one order per line at a time rhythm spends about what intake earns (about 34 per day against 35). Manpower at the chosen tuning is a reserve and expansion constraint: it binds a player who queues ahead or expands lines faster than intake, not the measured harness rhythm. No tuning slice was made to force profile binding: no ruling band broke, and tightening the numbers until the profiles bind would have been a rebalance in search of a trace, not a fix for a broken band.

The other new pins hold under it, correctly: the accrual test pins the player's accrual and both nations' intake formula through the readout (which the mutant does not touch), and the payment, gate, binding, and retool pins measure the queue path the mutant does not touch. The mutant was verified clean against the pre step suite first (73 pass, 0 fail), so the single failure is this step's pin and nothing older.

Every ruling band passes, so no tuning slice was made.

### Lessons
- A parity mutant fails exactly one test only if the rest of the section is shaped around it: the accrual test deliberately pins the Dominion's intake through the shared readout, not through incomePhase, so the intake only for the player mutant has exactly one pin to break, the tick parity test. Designing the mutant and the test split together, before writing either, is what makes the recorded count (79 pass, 1 fail) evidence rather than luck.
- A mechanic can bind honestly and still never bind in the harness profiles: the profiles order one unit per line at a time, which spends manpower at almost exactly the intake rate, so three cycles of balance reruns are byte identical while a queue ahead player is refused on the 14th order. The binding trace has to use a play pattern the profiles do not, and the log has to say the profiles do not bind, instead of tuning the numbers until they do.

### Judge verdict (fresh Muse Wonder subagent, no builder context; full report in hand)
- VERDICT: PASS. No fix-now flags, no conditional items, and none carried to Cycle 12 (so Cycle 12 has no step 0). The judge's deciding reasons: every claim in the Cycle 11 step 1, 2, and 3 entries reproduced independently, including the two claims this cycle lives or dies on, the binding trace and the non vacuity mutant; the constraint demonstrably binds, AI parity is proven through the real tick path, and every ruling band passes.
- The judge's own independent runs: node --check clean on all 10 JS files; dash byte scan zero; suite 80 pass, 0 fail, with the 7 Cycle 11 tests present and named as claimed; balance rerun byte identical to tools/baseline-cycle11.txt by diff (and that baseline byte identical to tools/baseline-cycle9.txt); the five browser checks at manpower 15 of 15, lore 116 of 116, efficiency 15 of 15, fort 17 of 17, and browser 6 of 6 in real headless Chromium, each rerun standalone with its own log after its first combined run interleaved and its exit markers were masked by piping, the same artifact the builder saw and resolved the same way. Ruling bands read from the judge's own balance output: passive reportDay 36 on all 8 standard seeds, inside the 30 to 45 band; sensible wins 8 of 8, all by enemy capital capture, reportDay 43 on 7 seeds and 28 on seed 2024, end strength 190.3 to 265.3 percent of the starting 72 with reinforcements counted and 33.3 percent counting only surviving starting units, so the 20 percent floor clears both ways; reckless loses 8 of 8 on reportDay 37; fortress wins 8 of 8 on sensible's days and strength.
- Code verification by the judge: every manpower reference in js/sim.js sits exactly where the step 1 entry claims (CFG manpowerStart 1200 and manpowerPerProvincePerDay 1, per template costs infantry 120 and drone 25 on CFG.unitTypes beside the steel costs, the readout family exported, the queue gate in queueProduction through the manpowerResult refusal helper carrying cost, pool, and shortfall and charging, queuing, and logging nothing on refusal, the full cost deducted at queue time on success, incomePhase as the sole accrual site for both nations, and createGame initializing both pools). The retool settlement in assignLineTemplate (charging a dearer switch per queued order, refunding a cheaper one, rejecting with a numbered reason when short) is confirmed as closing the queue drones at 25 and retool to infantry bypass; the judge considered it as a candidate flag for exceeding the prescription's letter and rejected that, because the prescription's not zero drone rationale requires it and it is the smallest rule that keeps one price per unit, fully tested, with steel behavior on retool untouched. No other rule reads the pool: no manpower reference in unitStats, stackPower, battle resolution, supply, any odds or preview computation, any steel or equipment price, or any AI movement code. The AI queues only through queueProduction, one order per idle line, with no discount and no second path. In js/main.js every displayed manpower number traces to Sim.manpowerPool, Sim.manpowerIntake, and Sim.manpowerCost, and the refusal notice renders the sim's reason in the open; one presentation note, not a defect: the nation card counts owned provinces with a local filter to fill its wording, while the intake number itself comes from the readout, and the two cannot disagree in substance.
- Binding trace, reproduced by the judge with its own scratch driver (public commands only, seed 42): 9 infantry orders paid on line 1 (pool 1200 to 120), then 4 drone wings on line 2 (13 paid in total, 1180 of 1200), and the 14th attempt returns exactly { ok: false, manpower: true, template drone, manpowerCost 25, current 20, missing 5 } with the recorded reason, queues standing at 9 and 4 and the final pool at 20, matching the builder's trace number for number and word for word; line 2 still had queue room and steel is never charged at queue time, so manpower is provably the only gate that refused. A parity spot check in the same driver left both pools at 1080 after one identical infantry order each, with one incomePhase adding exactly 35 to each. The judge confirms the honest scope rather than smoothing it: the harness profiles never bind (its own passive driver on seeds 1 and 42 shows the Dominion pool dipping to a post tick minimum of 1090 and ending at 2135, matching the recorded values; the builder's identical on all 8 seeds claim was verified on 2 of 8, consistent), because the one order per line rhythm spends about 34 per day against 35 intake. The prescription's binding test is a recorded trace through public commands while the ruling bands pass, the trace is real and pinned, and the scope is disclosed in every document; the judge records for the next judge that manpower at this tuning is a reserve and expansion constraint, not a tax on the measured rhythm, and that tightening it would be a rebalance in search of a trace, which step 3 correctly refused. The judge considered profiles never binding as its other candidate flag and rejected it for the same reasons.
- Non vacuity reproduced by the judge: the intake only for the player mutant (one guard line at the accrual site) gives 79 pass, 1 fail against the current suite, the one failure exactly the AI parity test (the Dominion accruing 1055 against the expected 1090), precisely as step 3 logged; and, attempted optionally and now recorded, with the mutant sim retained and the test file restored from the step 2 commit 936beb6 (suite 73), the mutant is clean at 73 pass, 0 fail, so the single failure is this step's pin and nothing older.
- Documents check by the judge: no contradictions between CYCLE_LOG.md, BACKLOG.md, and DECISIONS_FOR_JASH.md on Cycle 11; the four parked manpower taste questions are parked identically in DECISIONS item 2 and referenced consistently from BACKLOG, none was decided by the loop, and the judge verified in code that factory and fortification construction read equipment only. Older step scoped numbers are dated statements superseded by their own later entries, not contradictions.
- Compass grades for Cycle 11: HOI4 war heart FORMING, strengthened within the grade (the bible's compounding loop is now closed in its plain form: factories feed production, production feeds the army, and the army is fed by people the ground provides, so ground held is what raises an army and raising an army can run out; the grade cannot rise on a resource gate alone, with no new war verb, and what is missing for the next grade is exactly what Cycle 12 is for). Civ visible progression FORMING, low end, unchanged (the pool and intake are numbers on cards; taking ground visibly moves the intake number, but nothing on the map itself looks different, the same named limit as the last three cycles). EU5 lore STRONG, unchanged (no lore content this cycle and none needed; the UI speaks in the plain word manpower, with the flavor name question correctly parked for Jash). Legibility STRONG, with no remaining named exception (the system explains itself at the moment of use, on the nation card, the production rows, the unit card, and the enemy card by the same numbers, and a refusal that used to live only in a discarded return value now renders in the open with its real numbers).
