### Cycle 12 prescription from the judge, accepted and logged here
Cycle 12 is Drones and EW, with the cost exchange scoreboard, next in the deferred order the Cycle 8 judge set (after Cycle 12: intel and fog, then navy). No carried flag exists, so Cycle 12 has no step 0; it starts at step 1. The judge's honest statement of the current state, verified in code: research has exactly one item, Drone Gen 2 (flat researchPerDay 3 for both nations, cost 60 points, the AI decision made at the start of the tick on start of day numbers), raising drone wings from attack 3 and defense 2 to attack 5 and defense 3; a drone wing is an ordinary unit template (strength 6, 15 steel over 3 build days, 25 manpower) fighting inside stack combat like infantry; there is no interceptor, no jamming or EW mechanic, no air specific combat step, and no cost exchange record anywhere in the sim, UI, or lore. The bible's signature mechanic is therefore entirely unbuilt: a cheap drone forcing an expensive interceptor launch is a victory even when the drone dies, the scoreboard tracks who bleeds money faster, and drone generations and jamming generations counter each other through research. The cycle's one visible number: drone effectiveness percent in battle, a single percent computed by one shared readout from the two generation numbers, used identically by the battle, the attack preview, the stackPower estimate the AI decides with, and every on screen quote. The scoreboard's one headline number: net exchange in steel, value you destroyed or forced the enemy to spend, minus value you lost or were forced to spend.
- Step 1, sim: interceptor launches, the exchange ledger, and the EW generation counter. Interceptor stock, one shared path: each nation holds an interceptor stockpile in state, starting at a loop tuned CFG number, replenished only through a priced production path the loop chooses and records (a third production template whose completion adds to the stockpile instead of deploying a map unit, or a queueProduction priced stock order are both acceptable shapes; an instant free or steel only at click purchase that bypasses the production and manpower discipline of the existing economy is not). Interceptors carry one CFG steel cost, and the tuning shape is prescribed: one interceptor's full cost (steel, plus manpower if the chosen path charges it) must exceed one drone wing's full cost (15 steel, 25 manpower) by a wide, stated margin, because the exchange asymmetry is the mechanic; the exact multiple is loop tuning, recorded in the log with the arithmetic shown. The launch rule, inside the shared combat path and nowhere else: when a battle is fought in which the attacking side fields at least one drone wing, the defending nation automatically launches interceptors, one per attacking drone wing, up to its stock on hand; each launch consumes one interceptor, is valued at the interceptor's CFG cost in the ledger, and destroys its target drone wing outright, removed before or as the battle resolves by a stated, deterministic rule the step's tests can pin (the drone dies to the interceptor, not to battle damage, and is never counted twice). No drones in the attack means no launches, ever; a defender with zero stock launches nothing and the battle proceeds exactly as today. Whether the drone's own attack still counts in the round it dies is a sim shape decision the loop makes, states in the log, and pins in a test; it must be the same rule for both nations and visible in the preview (step 2). Launching is automatic in this cycle: a player toggle is taste, parked below, not built. The exchange ledger, record only: state gains a per nation exchange record, accrued at the moment each fact happens, in the beats and textures recording pattern: steel value of enemy units destroyed by your forces, steel value of your units the enemy destroyed, steel value of interceptor launches you forced the enemy to make, and steel value of launches the enemy forced you to make. Unit value is the template's CFG steel cost, read from the same CFG the production panel quotes, never a second price list. A drone killed by an interceptor counts once, as a destroyed unit for its owner and as a forced launch for the defender, and both entries are recorded because both are true; the readout defines the net so nothing double counts inside the net itself. Shared readouts exported in the readout family pattern (per nation destroyed, lost, forced, suffered, and net). No rule reads the ledger: no price, odds estimate, AI decision, research choice, or win check may consult it in this cycle; it is a scoreboard, not a currency, and the win and lose conditions are unchanged. The EW arms race, through the existing research path: exactly two new research items, bought with the same researchPoints pool and the same start of day decision shape as Drone Gen 2, for both nations at identical costs. (a) Drone Gen 3, requiring Drone Gen 2, raising the nation's drone generation from 2 to 3 (a nation that has not researched Gen 2 is generation 1; Gen 2 makes it 2); whether Gen 3 also changes the drone wing's printed attack and defense is loop tuning, stated in the log; the generation number itself is the counter that matters. (b) Jamming Gen 1, then Jamming Gen 2, in order, raising the nation's jamming generation from 0. The counter rule is one formula in one shared readout: a drone wing's effectiveness percent in a battle is 100 plus a loop tuned step (the prescribed shape is 25) times (its nation's drone generation minus the opposing nation's jamming generation), clamped to a stated floor and cap (the prescribed shape is 50 to 150). The readout multiplies the drone wing's attack and defense inside unitStats, so stackPower, resolveBattle, the preview, and the AI's shared estimate all see the same number by construction, and no other unit type is touched by it. Interceptors are not modified by jamming in this cycle (a stated limit, not an oversight: the generation counter governs drone fighting power; whether jamming should also blunt launches is parked below). AI parity: the Dominion lives under every part of this identically; it launches from the same stock under the same rule when the player attacks with drones, its launches and losses enter its ledger by the same code, it buys interceptors through the same priced path, and it makes its research choices (Gen 3, jamming) through the existing start of day aiResearch decision on start of day numbers, with any pacing or preference rule stated in the log as a spending rule, never a discount or a private price; its drone effectiveness is computed by the same readout against the player's jamming generation. Exit criteria: node check clean on every JS file, dash byte scan zero, existing suite green except where a test legitimately pins pre EW drone battle behavior (any such change named in the log with its reason), and builder spot checks showing: a staged attack with drones against a stocked defender launches exactly the stated number of interceptors, costs the defender the stated steel in the ledger, and kills the drones by the interceptor rule; the same attack with infantry only launches none; the same attack against an empty stock launches none; the effectiveness readout returns the exact stated percent at generation differences of minus 1, 0, and plus 1; and AI parity in isolation (identical staged battle with nations swapped gives mirrored ledger and launch numbers).
- Step 2, UI: the scoreboard and the EW state, legible at the moment of use. A scoreboard surface in the existing panel structure: both nations' exchange figures from the readouts, destroyed, lost, launches forced and suffered with their steel values, and the net exchange line in plain words that states who is bleeding faster and by how much. The figures update every render from the readouts only. The attack preview gains the drone lines in the meeting battle pattern: if the selected stack contains drone wings and the defender holds interceptor stock, the preview states how many launches the attack would trigger, what they cost the defender, and that the targeted drones die to them, plus the drone effectiveness percent for the battle with the two generation numbers that produced it (your drone generation against their jamming generation, and the reverse where relevant); a preview that hides either number fails this step. Research surfaces: the research area shows Drone Gen 3 and both jamming generations with their point costs, prerequisites, and current state, in the Drone Gen 2 button pattern; the unit card for a drone wing states its nation's current drone generation; the enemy card shows the Dominion's drone generation, jamming generation, and interceptor stock by the same readouts, so the arms race reads as a shared race, not a player menu. Exit criteria: a new browser check in the established pattern (tools/drone-ew-check.js or an extension of an existing check, the loop's choice, recorded) asserting the scoreboard's figures after a staged exchange, the preview's launch and effectiveness lines, and the enemy card's generation and stock numbers, with every expectation computed from the sim readouts and CFG, not hardcoded; all prior browser checks still pass at their recorded totals (manpower 15, lore 116, efficiency 15, fort 17, browser 6, unless a check is legitimately extended and its new total recorded); no horizontal scroll at 390 px; suite green; byte identity to tools/baseline-cycle11.txt is not required of this step if it touches no sim code, and if it does, the step stops and the change belongs to step 1 or 3.
- Step 3, tests and balance: pin the exchange, prove it moves in real campaigns. Formal tests in the established patterns pinning: launches (one per attacking drone wing up to stock, none for infantry only attacks, none from an empty stock, the drone removed by the interceptor rule and counted exactly once in the ledger); cost asymmetry (one forced launch's CFG cost exceeds one drone wing's full CFG cost, by the margin step 1 recorded); ledger exactness (a staged battle's destroyed, lost, forced, and suffered figures equal hand summed CFG costs, the net equals its definition, and two identical staged battles give identical ledgers); the generation counter (the effectiveness readout at generation differences minus 1, 0, plus 1, its clamp at the floor and cap, and a battle fought through the readout dealing the readout's numbers, with jamming affecting drone wings only, never infantry); research gating (Gen 3 refused without Gen 2, Jamming Gen 2 refused without Gen 1, points deducted exactly, identical costs for both nations); and AI parity through the real tick path (the Cycle 9 and 11 pattern). One non vacuity mutant shown failing exactly its target test, counts recorded, and verified clean against the pre step suite (the Cycle 11 mutant discipline); the judge suggests, and the loop may substitute a sharper one: interceptor launches free for the enemy (not entered in its suffered ledger), failing exactly the parity or ledger test. The mechanic must be proven to move in real play, not only in staged tests, in the binding trace pattern: at least one recorded trace on a standard seed through public commands only, in which real campaign battles trigger launches and the scoreboard's net moves off zero, with the trace's numbers reported; if the harness profiles produce no launches, that is reported honestly with the reason traced, a scripted drone forward profile through public commands supplies the trace, and no tuning slice is made to force the harness profiles to bind; if a profile does produce launches, the per profile exchange figures are reported beside the trace. Balance: the full rerun on the 8 standard seeds against Jash's ruling, which is the exit bar, not byte identity (EW is a real combat mechanic, so numbers will move; movement is reported per seed and per profile against tools/baseline-cycle11.txt): passive capital falls on day 30 to 45 on all 8 seeds; sensible wins 8 of 8 through the public command path with at least 20 percent of starting strength left, reported both with reinforcements counted and counting only surviving starting units; reckless loses 8 of 8. Any broken band is fixed by one small tuning slice at a time (one CFG number or one pacing rule per commit), never a one shot rebalance, and wins must come from intended mechanics, never from a defect. A new committed baseline, tools/baseline-cycle12.txt, in the harness's own format. BACKLOG.md and DECISIONS_FOR_JASH.md updated with no contradictions, including an honest statement of what the scoreboard does not do (it decides nothing, pays for nothing, and changes no win condition).
- Step 4, judge: a fresh judge with no builder context, grading against the bible and this prescription, independently rerunning the suite, the balance rerun against the ruling bands and tools/baseline-cycle12.txt, and all browser checks including the new EW check; verifying in code that every exchange and effectiveness number traces to the CFG costs and the one shared readout, that the ledger is read by no rule, price, odds estimate, or AI decision, and that the AI launches, researches, and bleeds under the identical rules; and reproducing the step 3 movement trace and the non vacuity mutant. A pass requires the exchange demonstrably moving in real play, parity proven, and every ruling band met.
- Taste parked for Jash by the judge, not decided by the prescription: whether interceptor launches should stay automatic, or become a player choice (launch or hold stock) once the scoreboard has taught the exchange (automatic is this cycle's loop default for legibility, not a ruling); the interceptor's flavor, its name and whether it reads as a missile, a counter drone, or a crewed interceptor wing (the loop uses the plain word interceptor until Jash rules); whether the scoreboard should stay steel only, or a later cycle should price losses in a combined steel and manpower figure (steel only is this cycle's shape, because steel is the currency every asset already carries); whether jamming should, in a later cycle, also reduce interceptor launches or their accuracy (this cycle deliberately keeps jamming on drone fighting power only); and whether Drone Gen 3 should change how a drone wing looks on the map beyond its numbers (the Civ visible progression debt, recorded since Cycle 5, is Jash's call when he sees the generations on screen).

- A judge that checks its mutant against the pre step suite as well as the current one turns a non vacuity claim into a closed proof: this judge restored the step 2 test file in its scratch copy and showed the intake mutant clean at 73 pass, so the parity failure at 79 and 1 is this step's pin and nothing older, with no builder prompting needed beyond the prescription's discipline.
- Both sides hit the same combined browser run interleaving artifact this run and both resolved it the same prescribed way (standalone reruns with captured output before recording any count), which is the Cycle 10 step 4 lesson working as routine rather than as a rescue.

Next, in order: Cycle 12, Drones and EW with the cost exchange scoreboard, as prescribed above, starting at step 1 (no step 0, no carried flag), then intel and fog, then navy. No stop condition is met or approached: the verdict sequence is Cycle 7 CONDITIONAL PASS, Cycle 8 and Cycle 9 PASS WITH FIX-NOW FLAGS with their flags closed, and Cycle 10 and Cycle 11 PASS, with zero consecutive failed verdicts, and the build is green on every check run this step.

## Cycle 12, Drones and EW with the cost exchange scoreboard, Step 1 of 4: sim (2026-10-16)

Prescription: the Cycle 11 step 4 judge prescription for Cycle 12, step 1 (interceptor launches inside the shared combat path, a record only exchange ledger in steel priced from CFG, and the EW generation counter through two new research items feeding one shared drone effectiveness readout, with AI parity throughout). This run advanced exactly one step: step 1 only, the simulation. Step 2 (scoreboard and EW state on screen), step 3 (formal tests, the real campaign trace, and the ruling balance), and step 4 (judge) remain. Builder rule for this takeover: built directly by Muse in its own context, no Anthropic line call, no external model. Lessons applied this run: the PYTHONPATH workaround for gh.py and removing /tmp/ashes-gh-sync before fetching (sync in); the Cycle 11 step 1 lesson that a resource paid at queue time makes retool a pricing surface (interceptor orders ride the existing queueProduction path, so the manpower gate and the retool settlement cover them with no second pricing path); the Cycle 9 step 1 lesson that the existing suite bounds a new system's shape (the drone stats pin and the campaign count pins were read before the effectiveness formula was applied, and every suite change this step is a named repin of a pre EW pin, below).

### Sync in
- With PYTHONPATH set to /opt/muse/skills/custom-connector-setup/bin and /tmp/ashes-gh-sync removed first, master fetched cleanly and diffed identical to local HEAD 9373121, file for file. Nothing copied in, nothing overwritten.
- Working tree was clean at run start, so the concurrency guard passed.

### Tuning chosen by the loop (recorded per the prescription)
- Interceptor: CFG.interceptorStart 4 per nation. The priced production path is a third production template, interceptor, in CFG.unitTypes: 50 steel over 5 build days (10 steel per day) and 10 manpower paid at queue time through queueProduction. One interceptor's steel cost, 50, is 3.33 times one drone wing's steel cost, 15 (and its full listed price, 50 steel plus 10 manpower, exceeds the drone wing's 15 steel plus 25 manpower on the steel side by the wide margin the prescription requires; the arithmetic is stated here so step 3 can pin it). A finished Interceptor adds 1 to the nation's interceptor stockpile and deploys no map unit; the template's attack, defense, and strength are never used in a battle.
- The launch rule, stated and pinned by the spot checks below: inside fightRounds, the shared combat path that resolveBattle and every movementPhase engagement call, when side B fights in defense mode against a side A of another nation, side B's nation launches one interceptor per attacking drone wing, up to its stock on hand, before the first round. Targets are the attacking drone wings in unit id order. A launched interceptor consumes 1 stock, is valued at 50 steel in the ledger, and destroys its target outright: the drone dies to the interceptor, not to battle damage, its attack never counts in any round, and it is recorded once as a destroyed unit for the defender and a lost unit for its owner, never again as a battle death. Meeting battles, where both sides fight in attack mode, launch nothing; a defender with zero stock launches nothing and the battle proceeds exactly as before. The same rule binds both nations by construction, because there is one code path.
- The exchange ledger: state.exchange per nation, { destroyed, lost, forced, suffered } in steel, accrued at the moment each fact happens inside fightRounds. Unit value is the template's CFG steelCost (infantry 30, drone 15), read from the same CFG the production panel quotes. Battle deaths are priced from the strengths the rounds start with, after any launches, so an interceptor kill is never counted twice. The net readout is destroyed plus forced minus lost minus suffered, so the staged drone that dies forcing a 50 steel launch nets its owner plus 35 on that fact alone (15 lost against 50 forced, before the battle around it). No rule reads the ledger: no price, odds estimate, AI decision, research choice, or win check consults it, verified in code and by a spot check that stuffing the ledger changes no unit stat.
- The EW counter: CFG.ewStepPct 25, CFG.ewFloorPct 50, CFG.ewCapPct 150, in the one shared readout droneEffectiveness(state, nationId, opposingId): 100 plus 25 times (the nation's drone generation minus the opposing nation's jamming generation), clamped to 50 to 150. Drone generation is 1 at the start, 2 with Drone Gen 2, 3 with Drone Gen 3. One honest consequence of the prescribed formula, recorded rather than smoothed: at the start the difference is plus 1 for both nations (generation 1 against jamming 0), so starting drone wings fight at 125 percent, and the floor of 50 can never bind with the shipped generation ranges (the difference runs from minus 1 to plus 3, so the lowest reachable value is 75 and the cap binds from a difference of plus 3, where 175 clamps to 150). unitStats multiplies a drone wing's attack and defense by the readout, after the generation base stats and before supply and walls, so stackPower, resolveBattle, the preview, and the AI estimate all see the same number; no other unit type is touched. Jamming modifies drone fighting power only and never blunts a launch, the stated limit the prescription prescribed.
- Research: Drone Gen 3 costs CFG.droneGen3Cost 90 points, requires Drone Gen 2, and raises the drone base stats to attack 6, defense 4 (CFG.droneGen3), a loop tuning choice stated here per the prescription. Jamming Gen 1 then Gen 2, in order through the one researchJamming function, cost CFG.jammingCosts 50 then 80 points. All three are bought from the same researchPoints pool at identical costs for both nations.
- AI rules, both stated as spending rules at identical prices, never discounts: aiResearch buys one item per tick on start of day numbers in a save priority (Drone Gen 2, then Jamming Gen 1, then Drone Gen 3, then Jamming Gen 2; while an earlier item is unowned it saves for it and buys nothing later). The Dominion replenishes interceptors through the same production path: when its stock is at 1 or none and its line 2 stands idle, it retooles line 2 to Interceptors and queues one; once the stock is back above 1 and line 2 stands idle on the Interceptor template, it retooles back to Drone Wings. The player has no such auto rule; retooling a line to Interceptors is the player's own choice, with its UI surface in step 2.

### What was built
- js/sim.js only, plus three named test repins below. New CFG: droneGen3Cost, jammingCosts, ewStepPct, ewFloorPct, ewCapPct, interceptorStart, droneGen3 base stats, and the interceptor unit template. New readouts, all exported: droneGeneration, jammingGeneration, droneEffectiveness, interceptorStock, interceptorCost, exchangeRecord, exchangeDestroyed, exchangeLost, exchangeForced, exchangeSuffered, exchangeNet. New research functions, exported: researchDroneGen3, researchJamming. createGame gives both nations droneGen3 false, jammingGen 0, interceptors at the CFG start, and a zeroed exchange ledger. productionPhase completes an Interceptor into the stockpile with its own log line. fightRounds carries the launch rule, the ledger writes for launches and battle deaths, and a launch log line naming the count and the steel. aiResearch and the aiTakeTurn production block carry the AI rules above.
- A bug found and fixed inside this step, before any commit: the first aiResearch draft bought whatever it could afford in order, so at 50 points it bought Jamming Gen 1 on the way to Drone Gen 2 at 60, spent the pool, and the Dominion never fielded Gen 2 in a full campaign (measured: jammingGen 1, 55 points, no Gen 2 beat on seeds 42 and 31337). The save priority above is the fix, and the research clock and beats tests pass on it (Gen 2 beat by the enemy on day 21, as before).

### Test changes this step (each one a repin of a pre EW pin, named per the prescription's suite exception)
- The Drone Gen 2 stats test: unitStats now folds in the EW readout, so the pinned numbers are the readout's numbers (before research, generation 1 against jamming 0 at 125 percent: attack 4, defense 3; after Gen 2 at 150 percent: attack 8, defense 5, computed from the CFG base stats in the test's own comment). It legitimately pinned pre EW behavior.
- The busy movement scenario (the order independence test): both nations are staged with empty interceptor stocks, because the scenario pins movement and order independence, not the launch rule. With stock on hand the defending Dominion launches at the attacking drone wing and it dies before the battle, which legitimately changes which defenders take damage; the test's own assertions stand unchanged on the isolated scenario. The launch rule's formal pins are step 3.
- Two texture campaign pins: the passive seed 42 campaign now records 12 texture occurrences, not 10 (one more rail conversion, and the Dominion's first line reaches full efficiency on day 29 as interceptor losses keep its lines working). Both pins (determinism and the on off comparison) are repinned to 12 with the reason in the test; their determinism and zero effect assertions are untouched. Formal re-verification of campaign pins is step 3, as the prescription assigns.

### Test and balance results (run by the builder this run)
- node --check on js/sim.js, js/main.js, and tests/sim.test.js: all pass. Em and en dash scan of the whole repo as a python byte scan: zero.
- node --test tests/sim.test.js: 80 pass, 0 fail, run three times at this step's end. One honest note: the very first full suite run of this step, against the aiResearch draft that starved Gen 2, stalled past 120 seconds and was killed, and two shorter follow up runs timed out the same way; after the aiResearch fix the suite runs in under a second, repeatedly. ### What was built
- `tests/sim.test.js`: 10 new tests appended in a Cycle 12 section, no existing test touched, no sim or UI code changed this step. The section header records the mutant partition chosen before the tests were written: the mirrored parity test is the only test in the section that asserts an enemy suffered figure from a battle where the Dominion defends; every other launch and ledger test stages the Compact as the defender, or asserts a suffered figure of zero for an attacking enemy, which no launch can change.
  - Launches (2 tests): a stocked defender launches one interceptor per attacking drone wing up to its stock (stock 4 against 2 wings: both drones dead, the defender at full strength because their attack never counted, stock at 2; stock 1 against 2 wings: exactly one launch, the lowest unit id wing targeted first, the surviving wing fighting on and finishing a weakened defender, the launch logged with its count and steel); an infantry only attack launches nothing, ever; and the same drone attack against an empty stock launches nothing and the drone fights and kills as it did before Cycle 12.
  - Cost asymmetry (1 test): Sim.interceptorCost traces to CFG.unitTypes.interceptor.steelCost, one interceptor's 50 steel exceeds three drone wings at 15 each, the recorded margin is 3.33 times, a drone that dies forcing a launch nets its owner 35 steel on that fact alone, and the template carries a manpower price above zero.
  - Ledger exactness (1 test): a staged interceptor kill (an enemy drone against a Compact defender) records player destroyed 15, suffered 50, net minus 35, and enemy lost 15, forced 50, net plus 35, all built in the test from the CFG costs; the kill is counted exactly once (destroyed is the drone's 15, not twice that); the net equals its definition for both nations and the individual readouts agree with the record; two identical staged battles give identical ledgers; a plain battle death with no stock on either side prices at the infantry CFG steel of 30 for both the destroyer and the loser, with no launch figure moved.
  - The generation counter (2 tests): the readout returns 125, 100, and 75 at generation differences of plus 1, 0, and minus 1; unitStats carries those numbers (at 75 percent the Gen 1 drone rounds to attack 2, defense 2; at 100 percent it fights at its printed base stats); infantry is never touched by jamming; the cap binds inside the shipped ranges (Gen 3 against no jamming is difference plus 3, 175 clamped to 150); and the floor is pinned with a staged jamming generation beyond Gen 2 (difference minus 2 reaches 50, minus 3 would be 25 and clamps to 50), recorded in the test as clamp arithmetic, because the shipped ranges reach only 75 at the lowest, the step 1 honest note. The battle test fights the same staged battle under the same rng seed at Gen 1 and Gen 2: stackPower attack is the readout number (4, then 8), the Gen 1 wing dies leaving the defender at 5, and the Gen 2 wing destroys the defender before it dies.
  - Research gating (1 test): Drone Gen 3 is refused without Gen 2 with points untouched; refused again one point short with Gen 2 held; bought for exactly 90 with Gen 2 held and not twice; Jamming Gen 1 is refused one point short, buys in order (the first purchase is Gen 1, never Gen 2), Gen 2 deducts exactly the second CFG cost, and there is no third generation to buy; the Dominion runs the identical sequence at the identical deductions through the same functions.
  - The priced production path (1 test, both nations): retool line 1 to Interceptor, queue through queueProduction (cost 50 steel, 10 manpower paid at queue time, buildDays 5 quoted from the readout at the floor); the stock is unchanged through 4 funded production days and rises by exactly 1 on the fifth, no map unit ever deploys, and the steel drained totals exactly the listed 50.
  - Mirrored parity (1 test, the mutant target): the same staged lone drone battle with the nations swapped gives role identical records (attacker lost 15, forced 50, net plus 35; defender destroyed 15, suffered 50, net minus 35), the same stock spent (one interceptor from the same start), and the same post battle strengths.
  - AI parity through the real tick path (1 test): staged with Gen 2 held and exactly the Jamming Gen 1 price on hand, one real tick leaves the Dominion at Jamming Gen 1 with its pool at the day's research income of 3, proving it paid the full 50 on start of day numbers (aiResearch runs before income) and that the player, whose research is its own clicks at the same prices, bought nothing automatically; and staged with its stock at 1, one real tick leaves the Dominion's line 2 retooled to Interceptors with one order queued through queueProduction, the stock itself still at 1 (the build lands five funded days on), and its manpower pool at start plus intake minus the shared infantry and interceptor queue prices, no discount.

No profile logic changed.

### Lessons
- Role indexed records mirror directly, not crossed: the first draft of the mirrored parity test asserted that the enemy attacking record equals the player defending record, which is backwards, because exchangeRecord is already indexed by role (attacker and defender), so the same role in the swapped staging must hold the same numbers. The suite caught it on the first run (89 pass, 1 fail, exactly that test), the assertion was corrected to role for role equality, and the corrected form is the stronger pin: it says the ledger does not care which nation holds which role.
- A floor that cannot bind with the shipped ranges is still a pin worth writing, but only with its reachability stated in the test itself: the effectiveness floor test stages a jamming generation the research path cannot sell, and says so in its comment, so a later reader does not mistake a clamp arithmetic pin for a campaign claim. Same discipline as the step 1 note that the floor cannot bind, now carried into the test file.

- Code verification by the judge: droneEffectiveness is the only effectiveness definition (100 plus CFG.ewStepPct 25 times the drone generation minus the opposing jamming generation, clamped to CFG.ewFloorPct 50 and CFG.ewCapPct 150), applied to combat numbers only inside unitStats, where it multiplies a drone wing's attack and defense after the generation base stats and before supply and walls; stackPower, resolveBattle, movementPhase engagements, the AI estimate, and the attack preview all reach it through unitStats or stackPower, main.js quotes Sim.droneEffectiveness for display and computes nothing itself, and no other unit type is touched. Exchange numbers trace to CFG only: interceptorCost returns CFG.unitTypes.interceptor.steelCost (50), fightRounds prices each launch at that cost for both the defender's suffered and the attacker's forced, prices an interceptor kill at the drone steelCost (15), and prices battle deaths from CFG steelCost read from strengths taken after launches, so a kill is never counted twice; exchangeNet is defined once in exchangeRecord. The ledger is read by no rule: every exchange reference in js/sim.js is the header comment, ensureExchange, the recordExchange write path inside fightRounds, the readout family, the createGame initializer, and the exports, with no occurrence in any price, odds, AI decision, research function, or win check; in js/main.js the only readers are renderExchange display reads, and the scoreboard's own on screen explain states that it decides nothing. Launches happen only inside the shared combat path: the single launch block and stock decrement sit inside fightRounds, gated on defense mode, opposing nations, and at least one attacking drone wing, one per attacking drone wing up to stock, targets in unit id order, victims zeroed before the first round so their attack never counts; fightRounds is called only by resolveBattle and movementPhase, meeting battles pass attack mode for both sides and launch nothing, and main.js holds only a labelled preview computation. Jamming never blunts a launch: fightRounds contains no jamming reference, the launch count depends only on attacking drone wings and stock, and the UI jamming explain prints that limit, asserted in the drone-ew check.
- AI parity, verified by the judge in code: the Dominion launches through the identical fightRounds block (no nation specific launch code exists); aiResearch runs at the start of the tick before income in exactly the logged save priority (Drone Gen 2, then Jamming Gen 1, then Drone Gen 3, then Jamming Gen 2, returning after each tier so it saves for an unowned earlier item) through the shared research functions at the identical CFG costs (60, 50, 90, 80); interceptor replenishment in aiTakeTurn uses only assignLineTemplate and queueProduction on line 2 at the identical prices (50 steel over 5 days, 10 manpower at queue time), with no discount, private price, or second path.
- Movement trace, reproduced by the judge with its own driver (public commands only, sensible profile adapted from tools/balance.js, seed 42): winner player, reportDay 38; player exchange destroyed 405, lost 360, forced 400, suffered 0, net plus 445; the enemy record the exact mirror, net minus 445. Forced 400 is 8 Dominion launches at 50 steel, and the Dominion ends with interceptor stock 0 from a starting stock of 4, so at least 4 of those launches were replenished through its priced production path during the campaign: the AI replenishment path demonstrably fired in real play, not only in staged tests. The judge's per profile balance figures also confirm the mechanic moves everywhere: passive suffers 200 (its whole starting stock) on all 8 seeds; sensible forces 400 to 650 per seed against 0 to 100 suffered; reckless suffers 200 on all 8; fortress forces 400 to 650 with 0 suffered on all 8.
- Non vacuity reproduced by the judge: the launches free for the enemy mutant (one guard on the suffered recordExchange call in fightRounds; the launch still consumes stock and still kills the drone) gives 89 pass, 1 fail against the current suite, the one failure exactly the mirrored parity test (the Dominion defender record returns suffered 0 and net plus 15 against the pinned suffered 50 and net minus 35), and 80 pass, 0 fail against the pre step suite (test file restored from the step 2 commit 837bae0), so the single failure is this step's pin and nothing older. Both counts match step 3's logged counts exactly.
- Documents check by the judge: no contradictions between CYCLE_LOG.md, BACKLOG.md, and DECISIONS_FOR_JASH.md on Cycle 12 (suite 90, the 10 tests, the mutant counts, the seed 42 trace, the committed baseline with every band passing and no tuning slice, the sensible reportDay spread, the steel only scoreboard limit, and step 4 as the only remaining step all agree across the three files). The five Cycle 12 taste questions the Cycle 11 judge parked are all still parked undecided in DECISIONS_FOR_JASH.md item 2, and the judge verified against the code that the loop used only the prescribed defaults: launches are automatic with no toggle anywhere; the plain word interceptor is used everywhere; the scoreboard is steel only; jamming acts on drone fighting power only; and Drone Gen 3 changes no map appearance (the map chip is the same label shape at every generation, only its numbers change).
- Compass grades for Cycle 12: HOI4 war heart FORMING, strengthened within the grade (the bible's signature exchange is now real war economy inside the shared combat path: drones force priced interceptor launches, both sides bleed stock that only production replaces, and the Dominion visibly spends and replenishes in real campaigns; frontline bending and the remaining v1 systems are still ahead). Civ visible progression FORMING, low end, unchanged (generations are legible on cards, in the preview, and in live drone stats, and the scoreboard gives the war a visible running total, but a Gen 3 drone wing looks on the map exactly like a Gen 1 wing; that is the parked taste question, not a defect). EU5 lore STRONG, unchanged (no lore content this cycle and none prescribed; nothing in the new copy contradicts the codex). Legibility STRONG, with no remaining named exception (the preview states launches triggered, defender stock, steel cost, the death rule and that the drone's attack never counts, plus both effectiveness percents with their generation numbers; the scoreboard headline names who is bleeding faster and its explain states the net definition, the 15 against 50 arithmetic, and the steel only limit; the jamming surface states it does not blunt launches; the enemy card shows generations and stock by the same readouts).

### Judge observations (explicitly not flags, recorded in the judge's own framing)
- Stale backlog section: BACKLOG.md's Bible v1 systems not yet started list still carried the air layer with interception and the cost exchange scoreboard, and the EW arms race, as not yet started, and its long production efficiency entry ended by saying Cycle 12 step 1 is next. The judge recorded these as superseded inside the same file by the Done entries and the header, assigned no step, and named the natural cleanup as part of this step 4 documentation commit; that cleanup is done in this commit (the two shipped items are struck from the not yet started list and the efficiency entry's tail now records Cycle 12 complete and Cycle 13 prescribed).

### Cycle 13 prescription from the judge, accepted and logged here
Cycle 13 is Intel and Fog, next in the deferred order the Cycle 8 judge set (after Cycle 13: navy). No carried flag exists, so Cycle 13 has no step 0; it starts at step 1. The judge's verified statement of the current state: there is no intel, fog, recon, spy, agent, sight, or visibility mechanic anywhere in js/sim.js, js/main.js, or index.html. The map renders every province, unit, factory, and fortification in full for both nations; the attack preview gives the exact shared stackPower numbers for any defended neighbor at any time; the enemy card gives the Dominion's exact pool, intake, factory levels, open lines, fortified provinces, generations, interceptor stock, and exchange record for free. Drone wings, the bible's recon arm, are purely combat units and interceptor targets today: they see no further than infantry. Intel as sight does not exist yet in any form. The bible's framing, which is the law for this cycle: intel as sight; the spy network is drone recon plus agents; it lifts fog and finds soft spots; spying is how you see, not a side menu. Fog of war and recon must be legible on screen, explain themselves at the moment of use, and must not create Stellaris style opacity: every hidden thing must say what would reveal it, in plain words, where the player is looking at it. Theater scale and v1 scoped per the bible's out list: no world map, no diplomacy system, no agent characters or agent management screen, no counterintelligence game. The cycle's one visible number: for each province, its intel freshness, the day it was last seen live, with today for a province under live observation and never for one never seen; the headline surface number is provinces under live observation now (a count of the 70), shown where the player plans. The one shared readout: intelState for a nation and a province, returning live, remembered, or dark, computed identically for both nations from the same sight sources, and read by the map, the inspector, the preview, and the surfaces below, and no other rule.
- Step 1, sim: fog state, sight sources, snapshots, and the agent path. Fog is an information layer only in this cycle: it changes no combat, supply, production, price, odds, research, movement, or win rule, and no battle number; stackPower and every other shared estimate keep computing true numbers in the sim, and what fog gates is what the UI may show (step 2) and what the recorded snapshot holds; this limit is stated in the log and in BACKLOG.md, in the exchange ledger's honest limit pattern. Sight sources, one CFG number per parameter, identical for both nations: a province a nation owns is live, and so is every province adjacent to an owned province; every province holding a unit of the nation is live, and so is every province adjacent to one of its units; a drone wing extends sight one province further (sight range 2 from a drone wing's province, through the province graph, regardless of the owner of the ground passed over, because it flies), which is what makes drone recon a distinct verb from infantry presence and ties this cycle to Cycle 12's drone arm; an agent (below) makes its province live. intelState is computed from these sources by one exported readout family (intelState, lastSeenDay, isLive or equivalent shapes), with no second computation anywhere. Snapshots: at the end of each tick, in a stated phase in the tick order, each nation records a snapshot for every province live to it that day: the day, the owner, the fortification level and any fort work in progress, the factory level, rail presence where relevant, and the units present with their nation, type, count, and total strength. A remembered province shows its snapshot and its day; a dark province has no snapshot. Snapshots are recorded in state in the beats and textures pattern (a per nation, per province record), deterministically, and no rule reads a snapshot except the display readouts of step 2: no price, odds estimate, AI decision, research choice, or win check may consult intel state or snapshots in this cycle, verified by grep in step 4. The scripted AI's decisions are unchanged by fog in this cycle and it does not read intel; that is a stated scope limit for the AI, recorded in BACKLOG.md, not a hidden asymmetry, because the sight computation itself is identical for both nations and the AI parity pins below cover the computation and the snapshots. Agents, the second half of the bible's spy network, in the smallest v1 shape: an agent is built only through a priced production path in the Interceptor pattern (a production template whose completion adds to an agent stockpile instead of deploying a map unit, at a loop tuned steel cost over build days plus a manpower cost at queue time through the shared queueProduction path, all identical for both nations), and is placed only through one public command, plantAgent or its equivalent, which spends one agent from stock to make one chosen enemy or neutral province (any province the nation does not own) live to that nation from the next intel phase until the province changes owner or the campaign ends. One agent per province per nation; a second placement on the same province is refused with a numbered, legible reason; placement on a province the nation owns is refused the same way. An agent reveals its province only, not its neighbors (the drone is the wide eye, the agent is the deep one). Whether agents should ever be removable, killable, or reveal neighbors is parked below, not built. The Dominion builds and places agents through the identical priced paths at identical prices, under one stated spending rule in the aiTakeTurn pattern (for example, it plants on the player's capital row staging province when it holds an agent and that province is not already live to it; the exact target rule is loop tuning, stated in the log, never a discount or a free placement). Exit criteria: node --check clean on every JS file, dash byte scan zero, existing suite green except where a test legitimately pins full information behavior (any such change named in the log with its reason; the expectation is none, because this step touches no displayed text and no rule), and builder spot checks showing: at game start each nation sees its own ground and its border live and the enemy heartland dark, with mirrored counts; moving a drone wing to the front makes a province two hops away live that infantry at the same province cannot see; a snapshot records on the day a province is live and does not update on days it is not; an agent built through the priced path and placed through the public command makes its target live and its snapshot record, and a refused placement charges nothing.
- Step 2, UI: fog on the map, legible at the moment of use. The map renders three visibly distinct states for provinces not owned by the player: live (normal rendering), remembered (dimmed or hatched in the supply overlay's visual language, with unit chips replaced by the snapshot's ghost figures marked with their day, or province military detail suppressed if ghost chips are not built; whichever shape is chosen is stated in the log and the other is not claimed), and dark (a stated fog treatment under which geography, province name, and terrain remain, because the theater map is known, but owner color beyond the last snapshot, units, factories, forts, and rail state are not shown). Owned and live player provinces render exactly as today. A legend on or beside the map states the three states and the sight rules in plain words (your provinces and their neighbors, your units and their neighbors, drone wings see one province further, an agent watches its province), so the system explains itself where it is seen. The province inspector is the moment of use surface: for a live enemy province it shows the true current detail it shows today; for a remembered province it shows the snapshot with its day stated in its first line (for example, last seen on day 18, this may have changed) and nothing current presented as current; for a dark province it states plainly that nothing is known beyond the map, what would reveal it (bring a unit next to it, bring a drone wing within two provinces, or plant an agent there), and shows no military figures at all. Soft spots, the bible's payoff, surfaced where a decision is made: the attack preview for a defended neighbor keeps its exact shared numbers only when the target province is live to the player; when the target is remembered, the preview shows the snapshot's defenders, fort level, and day, labels them as last seen, and states that the true numbers are not known today and how to make them known; when dark, it shows no enemy figures and states the same in plain words. The player may still order any attack legal today: fog gates information, never orders, in this cycle. The scoreboard, exchange, research, manpower, and nation aggregate surfaces from earlier cycles stay as they are (they are war level aggregates, not province intel); whether they should ever be fogged is parked below, and BACKLOG.md states this limit openly. The agent surface: the production panel reaches the agent template by retool like the Interceptor path, the nation card or intel surface shows agent stock, and planting is done by selecting an eligible non owned province and using a stated Plant agent control in the province card, with refusals surfacing their sim reason in the panel notice pattern from Cycle 11. The intel headline (provinces under live observation now, of 70) sits on the nation card or the map legend from the shared readout only. Exit criteria: a new browser check in the established pattern (tools/intel-check.js) asserting, with expectations computed from the sim readouts and CFG and never hardcoded: the three map states on a fresh game, the inspector's live, remembered, and dark wordings with their snapshot day and reveal instructions, the preview's exact numbers on a live target and their absence with the plain reason on a remembered and a dark target, the agent stock and a placement through the real control making its province live on the map, and no horizontal scroll at 390 px. All prior browser checks still pass at their recorded totals (drone-ew 28, lore 116, manpower 15, efficiency 15, fort 17, browser 6), unless a check legitimately asserts a now fogged figure and is extended or adjusted with the change named in the log and its new total recorded; suite green; if this step needs a sim change, the step stops and the change belongs to step 1 or 3.
- Step 3, tests and balance: pin sight, prove recon moves the picture in real campaigns. Formal tests in the established patterns pinning: the sight computation (owned ground and its neighbors live, a unit's province and its neighbors live, a drone wing's second ring live where infantry at the same province sees dark, mirrored identically for the Dominion); snapshots (recorded with the true figures on a live day, frozen while the province is not live even as the true figures change underneath, updated on the next live day, freshness day equal to the snapshot day); the agent path (built only through the priced production path at the stated steel, days, and manpower for both nations, placement spending exactly one stock, refusal cases charging and placing nothing, the placed province live and recording snapshots); information only (a full campaign with intel recording on and off, if a CFG gate is built for the tests, identical in every field except the intel records, in the textures zero effect pattern; if no gate is built, the equivalent pin is that no staged battle, price, or AI outcome differs between two games that differ only in agent placement); and AI parity through the real tick path (the Cycle 9 and 11 pattern: identical staged positions give both nations identical intel states and snapshots with nations swapped, and the Dominion's agent spending rule fires through the shared paths at the shared prices). One non vacuity mutant shown failing exactly its target test, counts recorded, and verified clean against the pre step suite (the Cycle 11 and 12 mutant discipline); the judge suggests, and the loop may substitute a sharper one: drone wings sight range computed as 1 instead of 2 (drones see no further than infantry), failing exactly the drone recon pin. The mechanic must be proven to move in real play, in the binding trace pattern: at least one recorded trace on a standard seed through public commands only, in which bringing a drone wing forward lifts fog on a named enemy province that infantry alone could not see and reveals a soft spot fact the player could act on (a fort level, an absent or weak garrison, or a snapshot that changes an attack decision the trace names), with the before and after intel state and the snapshot figures reported; if the harness profiles never place a drone where its second ring matters, that is reported honestly with the reason traced, a scripted recon forward profile through public commands supplies the trace, and no tuning slice is made to force the harness profiles to bind. Balance: the full rerun on the 8 standard seeds against Jash's ruling, which is the exit bar: passive capital falls on day 30 to 45 on all 8 seeds; sensible wins 8 of 8 through the public command path with at least 20 percent of starting strength left, reported both with reinforcements counted and counting only surviving starting units; reckless loses 8 of 8. Because fog changes no rule in this prescription, the expected result is a rerun byte identical to tools/baseline-cycle12.txt except for any intel fields the harness report legitimately gains (in the Cycle 12 step 3 exchange fields pattern, added only if a trace claim needs them); any other moved field is a mechanical effect and a step failure until removed or explained as a named, prescribed change. A new committed baseline, tools/baseline-cycle13.txt, in the harness's own format if the report shape changes, otherwise a recorded byte identity to the Cycle 12 baseline and no new file claimed. BACKLOG.md and DECISIONS_FOR_JASH.md updated with no contradictions, including the honest limits (fog gates information, not orders or battle outcomes; the scripted AI does not read intel; the aggregate panels are not fogged).
- Step 4, judge: a fresh judge with no builder context, grading against the bible and this prescription, independently rerunning the suite, the balance rerun against the ruling bands and the Cycle 13 baseline state, and all browser checks including the new intel check; verifying in code that every intel figure traces to the one shared sight readout and snapshot record, that no rule, price, odds estimate, AI decision, research choice, or win check reads intel state or snapshots, that launches, exchange, and every earlier system are untouched in behavior, and that the AI builds and plants agents under the identical priced rules; and reproducing the step 3 recon trace and the non vacuity mutant. A pass requires fog demonstrably lifting in real play through drone recon or an agent, parity of the sight computation proven, every ruling band met, and no surface presenting remembered or dark information as current fact.
- Taste parked for Jash by the judge, not decided by the prescription: whether dark provinces should hide even geography, names, and owner color (this cycle's loop default keeps the theater map known and hides only military fact, for legibility); whether remembered provinces should show ghost unit chips on the map or snapshot numbers only in the inspector; whether the war level aggregate panels (enemy pool, generations, stock, fortified province list) should be fogged in a later cycle or stay known as general war intelligence; whether the scripted AI should become fog bound in a later cycle, attacking only what it can see, with the balance risk that carries; whether agents should be removable or counterable, gain neighbors, or get a flavor name and service beyond the plain word agent; and whether a later cycle should add a non combat recon sortie order for drone wings (holding position to watch), beyond this cycle's passive sight from wherever a wing stands.

- Both the builder and the judge independently hit the combined browser batch capture problem in the same step and both resolved it the prescribed way (discard the batch for counting, rerun standalone, quote only the standalone logs), which confirms the Cycle 11 step 4 procedural lesson as routine: in this loop, browser checks are run standalone from the start.
- A judge observation with a named home in the closing documentation commit is closed bookkeeping even when it is not a flag: the stale not yet started items in BACKLOG.md were cleaned in this step's commit (see BACKLOG.md), the same shape as the Cycle 10 wording note, applied to the loop's own stale list.

No stop condition is met or approached: the verdict sequence is Cycle 7 CONDITIONAL PASS, Cycle 8 and Cycle 9 PASS WITH FIX-NOW FLAGS with their flags closed, and Cycle 10, Cycle 11, and Cycle 12 PASS, with zero consecutive failed verdicts, and the build is green on every check run this step.

## Cycle 13, Intel and Fog, Step 1 of 4: fog state, sight sources, snapshots, and the agent path, sim (2026-10-16)

Prescription: the Cycle 12 step 4 judge prescription for Cycle 13, step 1 (fog as an information layer only that changes no rule; one shared sight computation with one CFG number per source, identical for both nations; per nation snapshots recorded at the end of each tick that no rule reads; agents built only through a priced production path in the Interceptor pattern and placed only through one public command, with the Dominion building and placing through the identical paths under one stated spending rule). This run advanced exactly one step: step 1 only, the simulation. Step 2 (fog on screen), step 3 (formal tests, the recon trace, and the balance baseline), and step 4 (judge) remain. Builder rule for this takeover: built directly by Muse in its own context, no Anthropic line call, no external model. Lessons applied this run: the PYTHONPATH workaround for gh.py and removing /tmp/ashes-gh-sync before fetching (sync in); run browser checks standalone and quote counts only from standalone logs (the Cycle 11 and 12 procedural rule; the first lore log in this run's batch was truncated with no totals line and its standalone rerun is the count quoted below); the byte scan in python is the dash scan (zero below).

### Sync in
- With PYTHONPATH set to /opt/muse/skills/custom-connector-setup/bin and /tmp/ashes-gh-sync removed first, master fetched cleanly and diffed identical to local HEAD 2c8b5ad, file for file. Nothing copied in, nothing overwritten.
- Working tree was clean at run start, so the concurrency guard passed.

### Tuning and rules chosen by the loop (recorded per the prescription)
- Sight sources, one CFG number each, identical for both nations: CFG.ownedSightRange 1 (owned ground plus one province past it), CFG.unitSightRange 1 (a unit's province plus one past it), CFG.droneSightRange 2 (a drone wing's province plus two past it, through the province graph regardless of the owner of the ground passed over, because it flies). A planted agent makes its province live, that province only, never its neighbors (the drone is the wide eye, the agent is the deep one, in the prescription's own framing).
- Agent pricing, in the Interceptor pattern: a fourth production template, agent, in CFG.unitTypes at 40 steel over 4 build days (10 steel per day, kept whole) and 60 manpower paid in full at queue time through queueProduction, between a drone wing's 25 crew and an infantry division's 120. A finished Agent adds 1 to the nation's agent stockpile (nation.agents, starting at 0, so the priced path is the only source) and deploys no map unit; its attack, defense, and strength are never used in a battle.
- plantAgent, the one public placement command: it spends exactly 1 stock to watch one province the nation does not own, from placement until that province changes owner or the campaign ends; one agent per province per nation. Refusals are legible and charge and plant nothing: planting on a province the nation owns, planting a second agent on a province already watched, and planting with an empty stock (whose reason names the build price from CFG: 40 steel over 4 days plus 60 manpower). An agent whose province has changed hands counts for nothing everywhere, whether or not intelPhase has cleared the record, and intelPhase clears such records at the end of the tick.
- Snapshots: intelPhase runs in the tick after the dead are removed and before beatPhase, gated by CFG.campaignIntel in the beats and textures pattern. For each nation it records state.intel[nation].snapshots[province] for every province live to that nation that day: { day, owner, fortLevel, fortBuild (a copy of the work in progress or null), factoryLevel, rail (true if any edge touching the province carries rail), units (the units present grouped by nation and type, sorted, with count and total strength) }. A province not live keeps its last snapshot untouched. The readout family is the only sight computation: intelState (live, remembered, or dark), intelIsLive, intelLastSeenDay (today while live, the snapshot day while remembered, null while dark), intelSnapshot, intelLiveProvinces, and intelLiveCount. Fog gates information only in this cycle: no combat, supply, production, price, odds, research, movement, or win rule reads intel state or a snapshot, and the win and lose conditions are unchanged. The headline surface number for step 2 is intelLiveCount (provinces under live observation now, of 70).
- The Dominion's agent spending rules, stated, at the identical prices through the identical paths, never a discount or a free placement: placement, when it holds an agent in stock it plants it on the player's capital whenever that province is not its own and carries no agent of its own yet (an agent there watches durably, so the rule consults only its own stock and its own planted agents, never fog); building, it builds at most one agent, only while it holds none in stock, has none planted, and has no line on the Agent template already, and only on a line standing idle while its field army and queue reach the same 14 unit cap its general queueing works to, so the order displaces no combat order, and only when its manpower pool pays the shared 60 in full. Two guards keep the rules from converting its army pipeline into an agent pipeline: the build guard above, and a restore rule that returns an idle Agent template line to Drone Wings once an agent is planted or in stock, so the general queueing never orders agents by accident. The scripted AI is not fog bound in this cycle and reads no intel state and no snapshot for any decision, the prescription's stated scope limit, recorded in BACKLOG.md.

### A defect found and fixed inside this step, before any commit
- The first draft of the build rule had no line on the Agent template guard. In the first balance trace (passive seed 7) it queued a second agent on day 23 and a third on day 24 on the other lines standing idle at the army cap, because an agent order in production left the stock at 0 and no agent planted, so every condition still read true; three of the Dominion's lines were building agents by day 24 and its queued total read 17 against the 14 cap (queuedUnits counts a queued agent like any order). The guard (no build while any line is on the Agent template) closes it: after the fix the same campaign builds exactly one agent, on day 22 on seed 7, and plants it on the player capital on day 26 (seed 42: built once, planted day 24), the line is restored to a combat template, and the agent expires when the capital falls, exactly as the owner change rule states. This is recorded as the step's own found defect, with its trace, rather than smoothed over.

### What was built (commit c6c8720)
- js/sim.js only. Header note for the cycle; CFG additions (ownedSightRange, unitSightRange, droneSightRange, campaignIntel) and the agent unit template; the intel readout family and helpers (ensureIntel, liveProvinceSet through the graph BFS addSight, liveAgents with the owner change validity rule, snapshotFor, intelPhase, plantAgent, agentStock, agentCost); createGame gains state.intel (empty snapshots and agents per nation) and a zero agent stockpile per nation; productionPhase completes an Agent into the stockpile with its own log line in the Interceptor pattern; aiTakeTurn gains the placement, restore, and build rules above; tick calls intelPhase after the dead are removed; the new readouts and commands are exported. No UI, lore, test, or harness code changed this step.

- node --check on js/sim.js, js/main.js, js/lore.js, tests/sim.test.js, and every file in tools: all pass. Em and en dash scan of the whole repo as a python byte scan: zero.
- node --test tests/sim.test.js: 90 pass, 0 fail, the suite unchanged this step, as the prescription expects for step 1 (no test pins full information behavior in the sim, and none needed changing; the formal intel tests are step 3).

### Shape choices made by the loop (recorded per the prescription)
- Remembered provinces on the map show ghost unit chips (the prescription's first offered shape): the snapshot's unit groups as dashed chips, each marked with its snapshot day ("Day 1 ghost: Inf 12hp"), over the snapshot's owner color dimmed by a dark hatch in the supply overlay's repeating gradient language. The alternative shape (province military detail suppressed on the map, snapshot numbers only in the inspector) was not built and is not claimed. The choice is a loop default under the prescription, flagged for Jash in DECISIONS_FOR_JASH.md as still open for rewrite or veto.
- A remembered tile shows the snapshot's factory and fort badges, but no per edge rail bars: a snapshot records rail only as a boolean (whether any touching edge carries rail), so per edge bars would invent data the snapshot does not hold. The inspector states the boolean in words ("Rail when last seen: rail touched this province"). Dark tiles suppress rail bars as the prescription requires, and live tiles draw them exactly as before.
- The live inspector keeps every line it showed before this cycle and gains one first line ("Intel: live, seen today (day N). What follows is current."), so no prior wording moved; the remembered inspector's first line is the prescription's shape ("Last seen on day N: this may have changed since."), and the dark inspector's body is the plain statement plus the reveal instructions, with no military figures at all.
- One honest reachability note on the preview gating: under the shipped sight ranges (a unit sees its own province and one past it), a defended neighbor of a selected player unit is always live, because the selected unit itself sees it. The remembered and dark preview branches are therefore not reachable in unmodified play today; they are built exactly as prescribed, they are pinned by the new check through stated staging (one recorded intel phase, then the three CFG sight ranges zeroed in the served game only), and they become reachable the moment any sight range shrinks. This is recorded here and in the check header rather than letting a green check imply natural reachability (the Cycle 7 reachability lesson).

### A defect found and fixed inside this step, before any commit
- The production panel enumerated its retool targets by name (['infantry', 'drone', 'interceptor']), a list written when three templates existed. Step 1 added the Agent template to CFG, but the panel would never have offered a retool to Agent, so the priced agent path had no player route to it: the Cycle 12 step 2 defect class, one cycle later, in the same panel. The fix derives the list from Object.keys(Sim.CFG.unitTypes), so a future template appears in the panel by construction. No check asserted this before; tools/intel-check.js now pins "Retool to Agent" from CFG (and the drone-ew check's Interceptor pin still passes, since the derived list keeps the same order).

- The 8 tests: (1) sight computation: owned ground and the enemy front province across the border live for both nations, each nation's own capital live and the enemy capital dark with a null last seen day and no snapshot, mirrored live counts on identical starting ground, and the live count strictly between the 35 owned provinces and the full 70. (2) The drone recon pin, the mutant target: the front drone wing at p4_3 makes p6_3, two hops east, live, mirrored by the Dominion drone at p5_3 making p3_3 live; with both nations' drone wings grounded by a checker edit, infantry on the same provinces still sees one hop and no further, mirrored. (3) Snapshots record the true figures on a live day: after one intelPhase, p5_3's snapshot carries its day, its enemy owner, fort level 0, and its units grouped sorted (one drone wing at 6, one infantry at 12), and the dark enemy capital records nothing. (4) A snapshot freezes while its province is not live: with sight of p5_3 staged away (the border province flipped and its Compact units fallen back, both checker edits), the truth underneath changes (fort level raised to 2, the garrison moved out) and a later intelPhase leaves the day 1 snapshot byte for byte untouched, the state reads remembered, and freshness stays at day 1; when sight returns, the next phase records the new truth on the new day (fort level 2, no units, last seen that day). (5) The agent path, priced, for both nations: agentCost traces to CFG (40 steel, 4 build days, 60 manpower, all pinned), a nation starts with zero stock, queueing through queueProduction on a retooled line pays the 60 manpower in full at queue time, and productionPhase completes the agent on exactly the 4th funded day, draining exactly 40 steel in total, adding exactly one stock, and deploying no map unit. (6) plantAgent: an empty stock is refused with a reason naming the build price from CFG (40 steel over 4 days, 60 manpower) and charges nothing; an own province placement is refused and plants nothing; a placement on the enemy capital spends exactly one stock, makes only that province live (its neighbor p8_3 stays dark), and records its snapshot with the true capital garrison; a duplicate placement is refused and spends nothing. (7) Information only: a full passive campaign on seed 42 with CFG.campaignIntel on and off is identical in every field except state.intel (the comparison strips state.intel and nothing else, and compares the whole remaining state, units, provinces, nations, beats, textures, exchange, and log); with recording off both nations' snapshots are empty. One precision the test states in its own comment: campaignIntel gates snapshot recording and the end of tick clearing of expired agent records inside intelPhase; planting is plantAgent's own record and is not gated, so the planted agent record is present in both runs. (8) AI parity through the real tick path: after one tick both nations hold equal live counts and their mirrored front snapshots record the same garrison shape on the same day (each record labels its own nation, so the comparison normalizes the nation field and says so); and in a passive seed 42 campaign run on real ticks only, the Dominion queues its agent at the shared price its log names (40 steel over 4 days, 60 manpower), completes it, plants it on the Compact capital through plantAgent, and that capital is live to the Dominion through its agent, with its stock back at zero.

### Two test defects found and fixed inside this step, before any commit
- The first draft of test 7 asserted the recording off campaign's whole state.intel was empty. That assertion was wrong, and the sim was right: the Dominion's planted agent record lives in state.intel by design (plantAgent writes it whether or not snapshot recording is gated), so the off campaign carries the planted record and no snapshots. The builder verified the underlying claim directly before changing the test: with the intel records stripped, the on and off campaigns differ in no field at all, including the full log. The test now pins exactly that, with the gating precision stated in its comment.
- The first draft of test 8 compared the mirrored snapshots' unit lists with deepStrictEqual, nation fields included, which can never hold for mirrored records (each labels its own nation). The comparison now normalizes the nation field out and says why in the assertion text. Both fixes are to this step's own new tests; no sim code and no existing test changed.

### Non vacuity (scratch copy in /tmp, counts recorded as measured)

Against the current suite: 97 pass, 1 fail, the one failure exactly the drone recon test. Against the pre step suite (the scratch copy with the test file restored from the step 2 commit d9225b8, suite 90): 90 pass, 0 fail, so the single failure is this step's pin and nothing older, the Cycle 11 and 12 mutant discipline closed in full.

### Recon trace (recorded this run, seed 42, public commands only: issueOrder plus tick)
- Before: Drafeld (p6_2, the enemy province two hops north east of the front, behind the northern front province p5_2) is dark for the player: intelState dark, intelLastSeenDay null, no snapshot. The player knows its name and nothing military about it.
- The recon move: the front drone wing at p4_3 is ordered north to p4_2 (a player province) and the game ticks once. After: Drafeld is live, and the intel phase records its day 1 snapshot: owner enemy, fort level 0, fortBuild null, factory level 0, rail true, and no units present. That is the soft spot the bible's payoff names: the province behind the northern front stands empty and unwalled. Its starting infantry had advanced west to p5_2 during the same tick, which p5_2's own snapshot for that day confirms (one enemy infantry at 12, factory level 1). A player reading the snapshot knows the northern second line is open in a way the map alone, before the drone moved, did not say.
- The infantry counterfactual, same seed, public commands only: ordering the p4_3 infantry division north to p4_2 instead and ticking leaves Drafeld dark (p5_2 is live, as one hop sight gives), so the lifted fog is the drone wing's second ring, not the ground or the move itself.
- The harness profiles were not asked to supply this trace: their scripts mass every non garrison unit on the capital row and never move a drone wing off it, so no profile ever puts a drone where its second ring opens a new province. That is reported as the measured reason, the scripted recon move above supplies the trace through public commands, and no tuning slice was made to force a profile to bind, per the prescription.

### Lessons
- An on and off comparison is only as honest as its strip: the first draft of the information only test both overclaimed (a fully empty intel record with recording off, which the planted agent record contradicts by design) and, in its first repair attempt, understripped toward a fudge (deleting agent stock fields from the comparison on a speculative theory). The measured fact was cleaner than either draft: strip state.intel and nothing else, and the whole campaigns match, log included. Verify the diff field by field before writing the assertion, and let the test state the one gating precision (what campaignIntel does and does not gate) in its own comment.
- A mirrored record comparison must normalize the label that mirrors: two snapshot unit lists from opposite nations can never be deep equal, because each record names its own nation. Comparing the mirrored shape (type, count, strength) and saying so in the assertion keeps the parity claim exact without pretending the labels match.

### Judge fix-now Flag 1, carried to Cycle 14 step 0 (the judge's prescription)
- The defect: in renderProvinceCard (js/main.js), the factory button is set unconditionally from the true province after the intel branches: its cost is CFG.factoryCostPerLevel (40) times (factoryLevel plus 1), its label reads "Build / Upgrade Factory (N eq)", and at max level it reads "Factory at max level". No intel state is consulted. For a remembered province it therefore shows the current level, not the snapshot level its inspector just labelled last seen; for a dark province it shows a current military economic fact the dark inspector body explicitly says is not shown. The exact current factory level is recoverable from the label (level equals N divided by 40, minus 1, with the max level text a third distinguishable state).
- Evidence from the judge's own browser probe (unmodified game, seed 42, no staging, 0 page errors): dark Khessograd (p9_3, true factory level 2, dark in the node mirror) renders "Build / Upgrade Factory (120 eq)"; dark Ostmarsh (p8_3, true factory level 0) renders "Build / Upgrade Factory (40 eq)". Clicking any dark province in ordinary play shows it. tools/intel-check.js passes 29 of 29 because it asserts the dark tile, the inspector body, and the preview, but never this button; the fort button beside it is safe (a constant text for non owned provinces), which is the in codebase precedent for the fix shape. The remembered half is verified by the code path (one unconditional computation serving every selected province); the judge did not stage a separate remembered browser probe for the button and said so.
- Why it blocks a clean PASS, in the judge's framing: the step 2 prescription requires the dark surface to show no military figures at all, and the step 4 pass bar requires no surface presenting remembered or dark information as current fact.
- Prescribed fix, Cycle 14 step 0: in renderProvinceCard, when the selected province's intel state is not live, the factory button label must be a constant carrying no level derived number (the fort button's constant label pattern). Behavior for owned provinces (always live) and for live enemy provinces is unchanged. Extend tools/intel-check.js with assertions, expectations computed from a node mirror and CFG as the check already does elsewhere: the factory button text is identical for two dark provinces whose true factory levels differ, and a remembered province's factory button shows the same constant rather than its current or snapshot level cost. Rerun intel-check standalone and record its new total; suite stays 98; balance rerun byte identical to tools/baseline-cycle13.txt (the fix touches no sim code).
