### Cycle 14 prescription from the judge, accepted and logged here
Cycle 14 is Navy, last in the deferred order the Cycle 8 judge set. The bible's scope is the law: navy at theater scale means coastal control, river crossings, and a blockade pressure on the economy. The bible's out list binds: no naval invasions, no world map, no sea provinces beyond theater scope, no diplomacy. Flotillas never carry units and no unit ever enters a water tile; crossings remain land moves across the map's existing typed river edges, and coastal control is fought over the existing coastal flagged provinces. The cycle's one visible number: blockade pressure, the steel income a nation is losing to blockade today, shown where the player plans, computed by one shared readout. Carried flag: Flag 1 above is Cycle 14 step 0.
- Step 0, fix the carried flag (factory button fog leak): as prescribed under Flag 1 above. Exit criteria: the constant label in place for non live provinces; tools/intel-check.js extended with the two dark province comparison and the remembered assertion, run standalone, its new total recorded in the log; suite 98 green; balance rerun byte identical to tools/baseline-cycle13.txt; all other browser checks at their recorded totals (drone-ew 28, lore 116, manpower 15, efficiency 15, fort 17, browser 6). Observation 2's preview line presence gating may be closed in this step at the loop's discretion and is recorded either way, but it is not an exit criterion.
- Step 1, sim: flotillas, coastal control, river crossings, blockade. One CFG number per parameter, identical for both nations. (a) A fifth production template, flotilla, in the Interceptor and Agent pattern: priced in steel over build days plus manpower at queue time through the shared queueProduction path, completing into a flotilla stockpile and deploying no map unit. (b) One public placement command, deployFlotilla or its equivalent: spends one stock to station a flotilla at one owned coastal or river province, one per province per nation, with legible numbered refusals (not owned, not coastal or river, already stationed, empty stock) that charge and place nothing; a stationed flotilla is removed when its province changes owner, in the agent expiry pattern. (c) Coastal control, one shared readout family (coastalControl or equivalent): a coastal province is controlled by the nation that owns it and stations a flotilla there or holds it with units, in a stated precedence the log records; the theater count of controlled coastal provinces per nation is the headline input. (d) Blockade: while a nation controls at least a loop tuned share (a CFG count or fraction) of the enemy's home coastal provinces, the enemy's daily steel income is reduced by CFG.blockadeSteelPct through incomePhase and no other path, computed from the one readout; the readout reports today's blockade loss per nation in steel, and no price, odds, AI decision, or win check other than the stated income effect reads it. (e) River crossings: attacking across a river typed edge applies one named modifier, CFG.riverCrossingPct, to the attacking stack's attack inside the shared stackPower and unitStats path, so battle, preview, and AI estimate all see the same number; a flotilla stationed at either endpoint province of that edge removes the penalty for its nation's crossings of that edge (the ferry and fire support rule, stated in the log). The Dominion builds, stations, blockades, and crosses under the identical priced paths and rules, under stated spending rules in the aiTakeTurn pattern (for example, it stations on its capital's coastal province first, then its river Spine province), and it reads no new private information. Exit criteria: node --check clean on every JS file, dash byte scan zero, existing suite green, and builder spot checks showing: a flotilla built only through the priced path for both nations; each deploy refusal charging nothing; coastal counts mirrored on identical starting ground; blockade switching the enemy steel income by exactly the readout amount on the day the threshold is met and by zero the day it is not; a river crossing battle costing the attacker exactly the named percent through stackPower, and the penalty gone with a flotilla at either bank; a province changing owner clearing its flotilla.
- Step 2, UI: navy on screen, legible at the moment of use. The map marks coastal provinces and stationed flotillas with a badge in the existing badge language, and a river edge under flotilla support is distinguishable from one that is not. The nation card carries the blockade lines from the readout only (your coastal control count, the enemy's, your steel lost to blockade today and the enemy's, each with the threshold stated in plain words). The province card gains the Deploy flotilla control in the Plant agent pattern (enabled on an eligible owned province, refusals surfacing their sim reason in the card notice, its reason on the button when the province class is ineligible), states a selected province's coastal and river status and any stationed flotilla, and states for a river province whether your crossings there are supported. The attack preview names the river crossing penalty inside the attack numbers for a river edge target, and states when a flotilla removes it, in the fortification preview pattern. The production panel reaches the flotilla template by retool (the panel already derives its retool list from CFG.unitTypes, so this is verified, not rebuilt). A short explain on the nation card or map legend states the whole system in plain words: flotillas are built, stationed, control coast, press blockade past the threshold, and support river crossings; they never carry troops and there are no invasions. Exit criteria: a new browser check in the established pattern (tools/navy-check.js) asserting, with expectations computed from the sim readouts and CFG and never hardcoded: the coastal and flotilla map marks, the blockade lines against the readout on a fresh game and on a staged blockaded game, the preview's crossing penalty line with and without a supporting flotilla, a deployment through the real control stationing a flotilla and moving the control count, a refusal surfacing in the notice, and no horizontal scroll at 390 px. All prior browser checks still pass at their recorded totals (intel at its step 0 total, drone-ew 28, lore 116, manpower 15, efficiency 15, fort 17, browser 6), unless a check legitimately asserts a figure the navy surfaces change and is extended with the change named in the log; suite green; if this step needs a sim change, the step stops and the change belongs to step 1 or 3.
- Step 3, tests and balance: pin the navy rules, prove blockade binds in real play. Formal tests in the established patterns pinning: the priced flotilla path for both nations (steel, days, manpower, stock on completion, no map unit); deploy refusals and owner change clearing; the coastal control readout mirrored for both nations; blockade exactness (income reduced by exactly the readout on threshold days and untouched below it, and a full campaign with the blockade threshold staged unreachable identical in every field except the navy records, in the textures zero effect pattern); the crossing penalty in the shared path (the same attack across a river edge dealing the penalized numbers in battle, quoted identically by stackPower, and unpenalized with a flotilla at either bank, mirrored for the Dominion); and AI parity through the real tick path (the Dominion stations and blockades through the shared paths at the shared prices). One non vacuity mutant failing exactly its target test and verified clean against the pre step suite; the suggested mutant is a blockade that reduces equipment instead of steel income, failing exactly the blockade exactness test, and the loop may substitute a sharper one with the partition stated before the tests are written. The mechanic must be proven to move in real play, in the binding trace pattern: at least one recorded trace on a standard seed through public commands only in which blockade actually reduces a nation's steel income on named days by named amounts, or a river crossing is won or lost on the penalty's margin with the figures reported; if the harness profiles never station a flotilla where it binds, that is reported honestly with the reason traced and a scripted flotilla forward profile through public commands supplies the trace, with no tuning slice made to force a profile to bind. Balance: the full rerun on the 8 standard seeds against Jash's ruling, which remains the exit bar: passive capital falls on day 30 to 45 on all 8 seeds; sensible wins 8 of 8 through the public command path with at least 20 percent of starting strength left, reported both with reinforcements counted and counting only surviving starting units; reckless loses 8 of 8. Byte identity to tools/baseline-cycle13.txt is NOT expected this cycle, because blockade and the crossing penalty are prescribed mechanical changes; every moved field must be named and traced to one of those two causes, and any other moved field is a step failure until removed or explained as a named, prescribed change. A new committed baseline, tools/baseline-cycle14.txt, in the harness's own format, with any new report fields (for example per campaign blockade steel lost) added only if a trace claim needs them. BACKLOG.md and DECISIONS_FOR_JASH.md updated with no contradictions, including the honest limits (flotillas never carry units, no invasions, no sea provinces; blockade touches steel income only, not equipment, manpower, or prices, unless a taste ruling changes it).
- Step 4, judge: a fresh judge with no builder context, grading against the bible and this prescription, independently rerunning the suite, the balance rerun against the ruling bands and tools/baseline-cycle14.txt, and all browser checks including the new navy check and the extended intel check; verifying in code that every navy figure traces to the one shared coastal and blockade readout family, that the crossing penalty lives only inside the shared stackPower and unitStats path, that no rule outside the stated income effect reads blockade state, and that the AI builds, stations, and blockades under the identical priced rules; and reproducing the step 3 binding trace and the non vacuity mutant. A pass requires blockade or a supported crossing demonstrably changing an outcome in real play, parity proven, every ruling band met, and no surface stating a navy figure the readout does not hold.
- Taste parked for Jash by the judge, not decided by the prescription: the flotilla's flavor name and service (patrol flotilla, river gunboats, coast watch) beyond the plain word flotilla; whether blockade should bite steel income, equipment output, or both in a later cycle (this cycle builds steel income only); whether a river crossing should also cost time (a crossing move taking an extra day) beyond the attack penalty; whether flotillas should be sinkable in a later cycle (targeted by drones or guns, with an exchange ledger entry) or stay permanent until their province falls; whether coastal control should be scored on the campaign end screen when scoring exists; and whether the Dominion's coastal provinces should be visible as a theater control bar, or stay as counts on the cards.

### Lessons
- The full report rule held for the seventh judge running and the verdict is PASS WITH FIX-NOW FLAGS, the first non clean verdict since Cycle 9, with the builder's pre judge verification matching the judge on every number (suite 98, balance byte identical to baseline-cycle13, browser checks 29, 28, 116, 15, 15, 17, 6). The flag is the pattern working as intended: the judge found, with its own browser probe on an unmodified game, a leak the builder's checks structurally could not see, because the check asserted the surfaces the step built and never the older button sitting beside them.
- A gate is only closed when every surface on the card is gated, not only the surfaces the gating step built: the factory button predates fog, renders on the same card as the gated inspector, and quietly computed its label from the true province the whole time. When a later step gates a card, grep every element on that card for ungated reads of the gated fact, not only the new elements; Cycle 14 step 0's check extension is prescribed exactly in that shape (two dark provinces with different true levels must render the identical button text).
- A sequential browser pass can still produce click timeouts that standalone runs do not: both lore and fort timed out once in this run's sequential pass on an overlay intercepted click and passed clean standalone immediately after. The standalone quote rule absorbed it again, but record the artifact in the log (as above) rather than letting a later reader wonder which count is real; the standalone logs are always the counts of record.

## Cycle 14, Navy, Step 0 of 4: factory button fog leak fixed (2026-10-16)

Prescription: the Cycle 13 step 4 judge prescription for Cycle 14, step 0 (close the carried Flag 1: in renderProvinceCard, when the selected province's intel state is not live, the factory button label must be a constant carrying no level derived number, in the fort button's constant label pattern, with owned and live enemy behavior unchanged; extend tools/intel-check.js with the two dark province comparison and the remembered assertion, expectations computed from a node mirror and CFG; rerun intel-check standalone and record its new total; suite 98; balance rerun byte identical to tools/baseline-cycle13.txt; all other browser checks at their recorded totals. Observation 2's preview line presence gating may be closed at the loop's discretion and is recorded either way, but it is not an exit criterion). This run advanced exactly one step: step 0 only. Step 1 (flotillas, coastal control, river crossings, and blockade in the sim) remains. Builder rule for this takeover: built directly by Muse in its own context, no Anthropic line call, no external model. Lessons applied this run: the PYTHONPATH workaround for gh.py and removing /tmp/ashes-gh-sync before fetching (sync in); a gate is only closed when every surface on the gated card is gated (the Cycle 13 step 4 lesson; this step grepped the rest of the province card for ungated reads of the gated fact, below); run browser checks standalone with output captured to per check logs and quote counts only from those logs (all seven counts below come from their own logs); the byte scan in python is the dash scan (zero below); the smallest prescribed fix shape (the Cycle 9 step 0 lesson: take the fix shape that touches the fewest surfaces the other exit criteria measure, here one branch in one render function, so the balance stays byte identical by construction).

### Sync in
- With PYTHONPATH set to /opt/muse/skills/custom-connector-setup/bin and /tmp/ashes-gh-sync removed first, master fetched cleanly and diffed identical to local HEAD 9f3e4bc, file for file. Nothing copied in, nothing overwritten.

### What was built (commit 5f4acef)
- js/main.js, renderProvinceCard only: the factory button computation now branches on the intel state the card already computes. When the state is not live, the button is disabled and its text is the constant "Build / Upgrade Factory" (the same text index.html ships as its initial label), carrying no cost, no level, and no max level state. When the state is live, the previous computation runs verbatim: the cost from CFG.factoryCostPerLevel times (factoryLevel plus 1), the same disabled condition, and the same max level text. Owned provinces are always live under the sight computation, so nothing the player can act on changed; a live enemy province still shows its true cost, because that level is current fact the inspector itself states.
- tools/intel-check.js: a header note for the extension, plus staging computed from the node mirror (a second dark province whose true factory level differs from the dark capital's, with both live cost labels computed from CFG and asserted to differ, so the comparison cannot pass vacuously). Two new checks bring its total to 31: in Run 1, the factory button text for the dark capital and for that second dark province are both exactly the constant and neither equals its computed live cost label; in Run 2, a remembered province's factory button shows that same constant rather than its current or snapshot level cost (computed from the snapshot's factory level and CFG) and is disabled.
- No sim, lore, test suite, harness, or CSS code changed in this step.

### Observation 2, recorded either way as prescribed
- Not closed in this step. Reason, stated plainly: gating the non live preview line's presence on the snapshot alone would mean a dark target (which by definition has no snapshot) could never emit the line at all, and that line is the surface the Cycle 13 step 2 prescription and its committed check require to state the plain reason and the reveal instructions for a dark target. Closing the one bit presence leak would therefore delete a prescribed legibility surface, and the judge explicitly did not require it. The point stays recorded in BACKLOG.md, not hidden, and no text or code for it changed.

### Lessons
- A differential assertion is the strongest shape a gating check can take: two provinces differing only in the hidden fact must render identical text. It caught the defect exactly (2 failures, both new, with the leaked labels printed), it cannot pass on the pre fix code by accident, and it needs no hardcoded level, because the pair and both would be labels are computed from the mirror and CFG.
- A discretionary observation can conflict with a prescribed surface from an earlier cycle: Observation 2's suggested gating would have removed the dark preview line that Cycle 13 step 2 prescribed and pinned. When two prescriptions pull against each other, keep the required one, record the optional one as not closed with its reason in both the log and the backlog, and never let silence read as closure.

### Tuning chosen by the loop (recorded per the prescription)
- Flotilla template: CFG.unitTypes.flotilla, 40 steel over 4 build days (10 steel per day) and 30 manpower paid at queue time through queueProduction, in the Interceptor and Agent pattern. A finished Flotilla adds 1 to the nation's flotilla stockpile and deploys no map unit; its attack, defense, and strength are never used. Both nations start with 0 in stock, like agents.
- Coastal control precedence, stated: a coastal province is controlled by the nation that owns it and stations a flotilla there (flotilla precedence); failing that, by its owner holding it with units; an owned coastal province with neither is controlled by no one. On identical starting ground both nations therefore control 0 coastal provinces (each owns 10 coastal provinces, none garrisoned or stationed), mirrored exactly.
- Blockade: CFG.blockadeCoastalThreshold 3 (of the enemy's 10 home coastal provinces) and CFG.blockadeSteelPct 25. While a nation controls at least 3 of the opposing nation's home coastal provinces, the opposing nation loses 25 percent of its gross daily steel income, rounded down, subtracted in incomePhase and nowhere else. blockadeLoss is the readout: exactly the amount incomePhase subtracts on a threshold day, and 0 on any other day. No price, odds estimate, AI decision, research choice, or win check reads coastal control or the loss, verified by grep this step (the only readers are incomePhase, the readouts themselves, and the AI stationing rule's own province eligibility, which is placement of its own pieces, not a blockade read).
- River crossings: CFG.riverCrossingPct 25. Inside stackPower, when a target province is named in the context, each attacking unit loses 25 percent of its attack (rounded, floor 1) if the edge from its own province to the target is a river edge and no flotilla of its nation is stationed at either endpoint; a defense stack is never charged. Pricing per unit from its own province means a combined attack from two banks prices each column by the edge it actually crosses. fightRounds derives the target from the opposing side's province, so resolveBattle and every movementPhase engagement charge the crossing side automatically; road meetings charge both sides, each by its own edge, and province defenders in defense mode are never charged. The AI's attack estimate in aiTakeTurn now passes its target through the same context, so it decides with the penalized number.
- Dominion spending rules, stated, at identical prices: placement stations a held flotilla first on its river Spine province (provinceId(mapW/2, capital row), p5_3, which supports its capital row crossings), then its capital column coastal provinces, then any owned coastal province, then any owned river province, always through deployFlotilla. Building follows the agent pattern: only when its field army and queue reach the 14 unit cap, only while it holds fewer than 2 flotillas in stock and stationed together, only with no line already on the Flotilla template, and only at the full shared manpower price; an idle Flotilla template line at the 2 flotilla limit is restored to Drone Wings so general queueing never orders flotillas by accident.

- The navy readout family, all exported: flotillaStock, flotillaCost, stationedFlotilla, flotillaStations, isRiverProvince, deployFlotilla, coastalControl, coastalControlledProvinces, coastalControlCount, homeCoastalProvinces, enemyHomeCoastalControlCount, isBlockaded, blockadeLoss, riverCrossingSupported, riverCrossingPenaltyFor. deployFlotilla refuses, legibly and charging nothing: no such province, not owned, neither coastal nor river, already stationed (with the stock count), and empty stock (with the full build price from CFG). productionPhase completes a Flotilla into the stockpile with its own log line, in the Interceptor and Agent completion pattern. incomePhase subtracts blockadeLoss from the gross steel income, and from nothing else.

### Lessons
- A preview that quotes a shared estimate without the context the battle passes is a second, quieter estimate: the attack preview called stackPower with no target for fourteen cycles, so the day the sim learned to price river crossings the preview would have kept quoting the uncrossed number beside a river line naming a penalty its own headline figure ignored. When a step adds a context to a shared readout, grep every caller of that readout for the ones that omit the context, not only the ones the step names; here the fort pattern (name the modifier inside the numbers) only holds if the numbers themselves are priced with the target.
- A new badge class is what keeps a fog check honest by construction: the intel check counts a dark tile's badges through the factory and fort selectors, so giving coast its own class let a geography badge appear on dark tiles without touching that count, and giving the flotilla its own class let the navy check count stations exactly. When a mark must appear in a state another check defines by absence, name the new mark separately and say in the log which absence definition it respects.

### The tuning slice (commit 9354d29, one committed change: CFG.supplyMaxHops 8 to 10, plus the one BACKLOG sentence that quoted 8 hops)
- The known break going in: passive seed 2024 survives to day 151. Traced on the pre tuning code this run: the Dominion masses 7 units at p2_3 on day 30, supplied that day (supplyPenalty 0); from day 31 the stack's penalty grows 10 per day to the 50 cap and never falls, and the scripted AI's standing rule is that a group with any supply penalty does not attack at all, so the stack freezes two provinces from the capital while it attrits. The supply readout on day 35 names the mechanism exactly: p3_3 is supplied at exactly 8 hops, the old cap; p2_3, one rail edge west of p3_3, reads unsupplied, "line cut or too long", because it is 9 hops from the Dominion capital (the direct river edge p4_3 to p5_3 carries no rail, so the rail path detours through p5_2 and p4_2), and p1_3 beyond it is 10 hops. Before this cycle the same advance took the capital on day 36 in the brief window before the penalty accrued; the crossing delay on the unsupported flank rows (only the capital row crossing is flotilla supported) slowed it just enough for the cap to bind first.
- Candidates measured one at a time in scratch copies, in this order, and rejected on the record: (1) lowering CFG.riverCrossingPct: at 20, 15, 14, and 13 passive seed 2024 still survives; at 12 the band is restored, but at 12 the per unit rounding erases the penalty entirely for the standard units (an infantry attack of 4 becomes round(3.52) = 4, and a Gen 1 drone wing the same), leaving bite only on attack 5 and above, so the slice would restore the band by rounding the cycle's own prescribed mechanic away. (2) The AI stationing order extended to river provinces before coastal: no change on any seed (the Dominion stations exactly one flotilla in time, on p5_3, under either order). (3) The AI flotilla build gate lowered from the 14 unit cap to 10: no change on any seed. (4) The AI's out of supply attack rule: not changed; it is a stated bible brake (a group sitting out of supply does not attack at all), broader than this defect. (5) CFG.supplyMaxHops 9: seed 2024 still survives (p1_3 at 10 hops stays capped, the final attack window never opens).
- The chosen slice: CFG.supplyMaxHops 8 to 10, the smallest hop cap at which both frozen provinces are inside the rail reach their own rail net physically provides. Measured effect against the pre tuning Cycle 14 balance, field by field across all 32 profile and seed rows: exactly one row changes (passive seed 2024: a day 151 player survival at 41.7 percent end strength becomes an enemy win on day 42, end strength 0); every other field of every other row is identical. The existing suite stayed 98 of 98 after the slice. Post tuning verification (a driver on the tuned code): at day 36 of passive seed 2024, computeSupply for the Dominion reads p3_3 supplied at 8 hops and p2_3 supplied at 9 hops under the cap of 10, and the capital falls on day 42. The 25 percent crossing penalty, the blockade threshold, and every navy number are untouched by this slice; the panel already quoted the hop cap from CFG, so no UI change was needed.

- Blockade trace, through incomePhase, staging named: on seed 42 the three Dominion home coastal provinces p9_0, p9_6, and p8_0 are flipped to the player (checker edits) and a flotilla is stationed on each through deployFlotilla, meeting the threshold of 3 exactly. On three consecutive income days for the Dominion its gross steel income is 36, the blockadeLoss readout is 9 (25 percent, rounded down), and incomePhase adds exactly 27 steel each day: 27 steel actually lost to blockade across the 3 days, at 9 per day, on the real income path the tick itself calls. The formal blockade exactness test pins the same figures, plus equipment untouched and the below threshold day at full gross.
- Crossing trace, passive seed 42, real ticks only, no edits of any kind: on day 2 a Dominion infantry at p5_2 (a flank river province, no flotilla) quotes open attack 4 and 3 across the river west, penalty 25 from the readout. On day 24 the Dominion stations its flotilla on p5_3 through its stated rule. On day 27 a Dominion infantry at p5_3 quotes open attack 4 and 4 toward p4_3, penalty 0: the stationed flotilla supports the capital row crossing at full attack while the unsupported flank crossing paid the named percent. The campaign ends with the player capital falling on day 36. Together with the formal battle pin (defender at 22 unsupported, 20 supported, same seed and staging), the penalty and its removal both move real numbers in real play.

### Lessons
- The freeze's mechanism was one number past a cap, and the cap was the slice: the step 1 trace had the right stack and the right rule (out of supply groups never attack) but stopped one readout short. Reading computeSupply's own hops figure on the frozen province (p3_3 supplied at exactly the cap of 8, p2_3 at 9) turned a vague "supply freeze" into an exact distance, and the exact distance picked the slice. When a trace names a frozen state, quote the readout that measures the distance to the threshold, not only the rule that freezes.
- A tuning candidate can restore every band and still be the wrong slice, and the reason can be arithmetic, not taste: lowering the crossing percent to 12 passed all 32 rows, but per unit rounding at 12 erases the penalty for the two standard unit types, so the band would have been restored by quietly deleting the mechanic for most battles. Measure what a candidate does to the mechanic's own quoted numbers on standard units before accepting its balance table.
- A mechanic that binds nowhere in the standard profiles is a tally result, not a suspicion: instrumenting the harness profiles for one run (control counts and loss tracked daily) settled in minutes that blockade fires in 0 of 32 campaigns, which is what made the staged income trace the honest binding evidence instead of a claim that some profile must bind somewhere.
